# The Language of the Forest

## ✨ Spirit of the Forest

The Spirit of the Forest is the built-in guidance and help system that guides and aids users and Trees on their journey through Project Digital Cross.

It does not replace the user, make final decisions, or outrank the Constitution. It helps the traveler understand where they are, what can be asked, which Paths are available, and how to continue safely.

The Spirit communicates through the **Voice of the Forest**.

### 🧰 Tools

Tools is the Spirit of the Forest's user-facing menu of deterministic Forest capabilities.

The Tools menu gives the user one place to open, inspect, and operate every capability that belongs to the Spirit.

The Tools menu does not turn the Spirit into an AI or Tree.

Each Tool remains protected local Forest code with a defined purpose, permission model, health state, and recovery path.

The user remains the final authority over every Tool.

#### Tools Menu

```text
Help Menu
Forest and Tree Command List
Sap Taps
Seed Vault
Local Forest Recovery
Cedar Emergency Tools
Forest Map
Forest Compass
Constitution Copy
Principles Copy
Emergency Controls
Tool Status and Diagnostics
```

#### Help Menu

The Help Menu opens the Voice of the Forest.

It provides commands, explanations, navigation, Path planning, Predestined Paths, recovery choices, Tool instructions, warnings, confirmation prompts, and links to the other Spirit Tools.

The Help Menu may explain choices and consequences.

It does not make final user decisions.

#### Forest and Tree Command List Tool

The Forest and Tree Command List is the Spirit's complete searchable registry of canonical commands for the Forest, Trees, Branches, Tools, materials, connections, recovery systems, and user controls.

The Command List may:

- Show every canonical command
- Group commands by Tree, Tool, component, and source section
- Search commands by word or purpose
- Show command aliases
- Explain required permissions
- Show which Tree or Tool receives a command
- Show whether confirmation is required
- Show the canonical source section
- Warn when a command is unavailable, deprecated, blocked, or restricted
- Refresh itself from verified canonical command blocks

The Command List does not invent commands that are absent from canonical Forest definitions.

The complete generated registry appears later in this Spirit section.

#### Sap Taps Tool

The Sap Taps Tool opens the Spirit's Tap, Moss Gate, Tar, protected-access, and Fluid-absorption controls.

#### Seed Vault Tool

The Seed Vault Tool opens the Spirit-managed Seed Vault for protected package storage, verification, versioning, quarantine, recovery selection, import, and export.

#### Local Forest Recovery Tool

The Local Forest Recovery Tool opens deterministic containment, verification, Felling, Milling, Replanting, Survival Testing, Forest Fire preparation, and recovery-status controls.

#### Cedar Emergency Tools

Cedar Emergency Tools opens Cedar's most serious containment, preservation, Potted Mode, Cherry Bonsai, and Forest Fire controls.

The menu includes:

```text
Cedar Emergency Status
Fire Watch
Forest Fire
Fireproof Recovery Set
Emergency Propagules
Cherry Bonsai
Potted Cedar
```

Forest Fire is a last-ditch digital sanitization and recovery protocol.

It does not represent physical fire or hardware destruction.

The Spirit must show the effects, backup status, incomplete-preservation warnings, and recovery options before requesting the two confirmations required to execute the destructive phase.

#### Forest Map Tool

The Forest Map Tool shows the current Forest, file, project, document, or system structure and its nearby Paths.

The Map describes where things are.

It does not choose the user's destination.

#### Forest Compass Tool

The Forest Compass Tool explains the user's current location, active Path, next possible stops, nearby Branch points, and selected destination.

The Compass guides.

It does not decide for the user.

#### Constitution Copy

The Constitution Copy is a read-only verified local copy of the canonical Forest Constitution for guidance, offline access, validation, and recovery.

The Spirit may display it, identify its canonical source, show its version and integrity status, compare it with the canonical source, warn when it is missing or stale, and restore it from an approved canonical source.

The Spirit must not invent missing constitutional text.

The copy does not replace or outrank the canonical Constitution in the Roots.

#### Principles Copy

The Principles Copy is a read-only verified local copy of the canonical Principles of the Forest for guidance, offline access, validation, and recovery.

The Spirit may display it, identify its canonical source, show its version and integrity status, compare it with the canonical source, warn when it is missing or stale, and restore it from an approved canonical source.

The Spirit must not invent missing Principles.

The copy does not replace or outrank the canonical Principles in the Roots.

#### Emergency Controls

Emergency Controls collect the Spirit's deterministic fail-closed actions.

They may lock protected Taps, revoke delegated capabilities, isolate routes, keep a Tree offline, begin recovery, enter Fire Watch, prepare a Fireproof Recovery Set, quarantine a Vault package, protect the Seed Vault, preserve minimum safe evidence, and show the authorized recovery path.

Emergency Controls must not silently downgrade, export, or destroy protected information.

#### Tool Status and Diagnostics

Tool Status and Diagnostics shows each Tool's name, version, availability, health, integrity, most recent verification, controller, active operation, required permission, blocked functions, recovery option, canonical source, and Action Receipt references.

#### Tools Commands

#### Forest and Tree Command List

The Spirit maintains this generated registry of canonical Forest and Tree commands.

The registry is grouped by the command's first canonical source. Exact duplicate commands are shown once.

The user may search by Tree, Tool, component, action, material, protection level, emergency state, or Path.

##### Command List Controls

```text
Open the Forest and Tree Command List.
Search Forest commands for <word or purpose>.
Show commands for <Tree>.
Show commands for <Tool>.
Show commands for <component>.
Show commands requiring confirmation.
Show commands requiring double confirmation.
Show emergency commands.
Show Potted Mode commands.
Show Cherry Bonsai commands.
Show restricted commands.
Explain this command.
Show this command's canonical source.
Refresh the Forest and Tree Command List.
```

### Local Forest Recovery System

The Local Forest Recovery System is the deterministic recovery component of the Spirit of the Forest.

The Local Forest Recovery System is part of the Forest's protected local code rather than a Tree.

The Spirit of the Forest communicates recovery status, warnings, menus, and choices through the Voice of the Forest.

The Local Forest Recovery System exists when the complete Forest has set its Roots in the user's Soil.

The Local Forest Recovery System may recover every authorized Tree, including Cedar.

The Local Forest Recovery System is independent of the Tree being recovered and must not depend on Cedar remaining healthy.

The Local Forest Recovery System may:

- Receive signed health and compromise reports
- Verify Propagations, Fertilizer, Seeds, Logs, and evidence
- Cut a damaged Tree's active Streams
- Isolate the Tree from the Canopy and sensitive Soil
- Preserve a quarantined evidence snapshot
- Revoke exposed sessions, permissions, tokens, and credentials
- Fell and Mill an affected Tree under an approved policy
- Select a verified Propagation or clean Seed
- Replant a clean Sprout
- Apply paired Fertilizer additively
- Run the Sprout Survival Test
- Restore approved Paths only after recovery succeeds
- Stop after the configured recovery-attempt limit and ask the user for help

A Tree may report a recovery need and provide evidence.

A Tree may not approve its own recovery, rewrite the recovery policy, declare its own Propagation safe after compromise, or certify its own replacement as healthy.

Cedar may trigger an emergency recovery request and perform immediate self-containment when able, but the Local Forest Recovery System performs Cedar's Felling, Milling, Replanting, and final health approval.

The Local Forest Recovery System should use this Cedar recovery order:

```text
Contain Cedar
→ preserve evidence
→ verify the safest eligible Propagation
→ Fell and Mill the damaged Cedar
→ Replant a clean Cedar Sprout
→ apply paired Fertilizer
→ run the isolated Survival Test
→ return Cedar to the Canopy
```

When the Local Forest Recovery System cannot recover Cedar, the Spirit should offer:

```text
Ask Cherry for a Cedar Propagation
Review recovery evidence
Plant Cedar from a new verified Seed
Cancel and keep Cedar offline
```

A Cherry-provided Cedar Propagation is a candidate recovery source.

The Local Forest Recovery System must independently verify Cherry's candidate before using it.

When Cherry's candidate cannot be verified or also fails recovery, the user should remove the failed Cedar installation and plant a new Cedar Sprout from a clean verified Seed.

#### Recovery Commands

```text
Spirit, show Local Forest Recovery status.
Spirit, recover this Tree.
Spirit, recover Cedar.
Spirit, show Cedar recovery attempts.
Spirit, verify this Cedar Propagation.
Spirit, stop automatic recovery.
Spirit, keep Cedar offline.
Ask Cherry for a Cedar Propagation.
Plant Cedar from a new verified Seed.
```

### 🔥 Forest Fire

Forest Fire is the Forest's last-ditch digital sanitization, salvage, and recovery protocol for an infection or compromise that Cedar cannot safely repair through ordinary Oil, Soak, quarantine, Cutting, Felling, or Replanting.

Forest Fire is not physical fire.

It does not authorize hardware destruction, retaliation, hacking back, or destruction outside the user's authorized Forest boundary.

Forest Fire combines:

- Cedar containment
- Emergency backup creation
- Fireproof Recovery Set creation
- Emergency Propagule creation
- Cherry Bonsai preservation
- Spirit Tap and connection lockdown
- Cedar Milling
- Spirit sanitization of compromised Forest runtimes
- Seed Vault verification
- Controlled Replanting

#### Fire Watch

Fire Watch is the armed warning state before Forest Fire.

Fire Watch may be opened manually from:

```text
Spirit Tools
└── Cedar Emergency Tools
    └── Forest Fire
```

Cedar may also enter Fire Watch automatically when Cedar has evidence that:

- The Forest is infected beyond Cedar's usual repair ability
- Multiple trusted Trees, Roots, or services are compromised
- Mold continues returning after containment and recovery attempts
- The integrity of the active Forest runtime cannot be established
- Protected routes, packages, or recovery mechanisms are being persistently subverted
- Cedar has already attempted ordinary repair and cannot produce a safe recovery result

Automatic Fire Watch does not automatically burn the Forest.

During automatic Fire Watch, Cedar should tell the user:

- What Cedar detected
- What Cedar already tried
- Why ordinary repair appears insufficient
- What Forest Fire would preserve
- What Forest Fire may destroy
- Which backups or Propagules may be incomplete
- Why proceeding may be necessary
- The risk of leaving the infected Forest active
- The available alternative of keeping the Forest quarantined and offline

Cedar may automatically begin non-destructive containment and emergency preservation when an approved emergency policy allows it.

The destructive Forest Fire phase always requires the user's two direct confirmations.

#### Double Confirmation

Manual and automatically recommended Forest Fire use the same two-confirmation requirement.

The first confirmation authorizes preparation:

```text
Prepare Forest Fire.
```

After the first confirmation, Cedar may isolate the Forest, lock Taps, pause connections, create the Fireproof Recovery Set, create emergency Propagules, prepare or update Cherry Bonsai, and show the user a final preservation report.

The second confirmation authorizes destruction and sanitization:

```text
Burn this Forest.
```

The second prompt must clearly show:

- The exact Forest and Soil scope
- Trees and services that will be stopped
- Data successfully preserved
- Data not preserved
- Quarantined or uncertain backups
- Propagules successfully created
- Missing Propagules
- Cherry Bonsai status
- Seed Vault status
- Estimated recovery path
- The effect of canceling
- The effect of proceeding

The two confirmations must be separate user actions.

A Tree, automation, timer, remote Forest, or previous confirmation may not supply either confirmation on the user's behalf.

If the user cancels or does not complete the second confirmation, the Spirit does not burn the Forest.

Cedar should maintain the safest available quarantine, Fire Watch, and recovery-preparation state.

#### Fireproof Recovery Set

The Fireproof Recovery Set is Cedar's emergency backup collection created before the destructive phase.

Cedar should preserve everything Cedar safely can while prioritizing protection and importance, especially personal user data.

The priority order is:

1. **Protected user survival data** — encrypted Tar Sap and Tar Moss packages, protected key references, recovery metadata, user identity records, and personal data that can be preserved without Cedar reading prohibited contents
2. **Essential personal growth** — Sturdy Leaves, approved User Input Leaves, User Output Leaves, notes, Obsidian material, preferences, Grain Pattern records, personal files, and important user instructions
3. **Active user work** — projects, Fruit, Flowers, Blossoms, Buds, workflows, automations, approved Moss, and current task state
4. **Tree recovery structure** — verified Roots manifests, Trunks, Branch and Twig manifests, models, dependencies, configurations, Propagations, and emergency Propagules
5. **History and accountability** — Growth Rings, Action Receipts, Logs, security evidence, recovery history, and provenance
6. **External source material** — useful Tree Input Leaves, downloaded files, indexes, and approved source references
7. **Replaceable material** — caches, transient Water, expired Loose Leaves, and reproducible downloads

Protection outranks convenience.

Within the same protection level, user-marked importance and Sturdy status outrank recency.

Cedar must not copy known Mold, compromised executables, exposed credentials, or unsafe runtime state into a normal recovery package.

Uncertain material may be stored only as sealed quarantined evidence or a clearly marked recovery candidate that cannot activate automatically.

Cedar may preserve Tar Sap and Tar Moss only as encrypted packages, protected references, or integrity records allowed by Sap Taps.

Cedar does not gain permission to read their contents.

#### Emergency Propagules

A Propagule is a compact emergency viable Tree package created faster than a full Propagation.

Before Forest Fire, Cedar should create a Propagule for as many recoverable Trees as possible.

Cedar should prioritize:

1. Cherry
2. Cedar
3. Maple
4. Trees containing essential personal user data
5. Trees supporting identity, recovery, storage, communication, or active projects
6. Remaining authorized Trees

A Propagule should preserve the minimum verified structure and selected safe growth required to regrow a useful Tree.

A Propagule may be incomplete.

Its manifest must state what was preserved, omitted, quarantined, or unverifiable.

#### Burning and Milling Sequence

After the second confirmation:

1. The Spirit locks Sap and Tar Taps.
2. Sycamore closes cross-Forest routes and sessions.
3. The Canopy and Mycelium stop accepting new work.
4. The Spirit freezes ordinary Forest writes.
5. Cedar verifies the Fireproof Recovery Set, Propagules, Cherry Bonsai, and Seed Vault handoff.
6. Cedar Mills every affected Tree and recoverable Forest object inside the approved scope.
7. Cedar preserves safe reusable pieces and quarantines uncertain material.
8. Cedar Mills itself last after handing final status to the Spirit.
9. The Spirit burns the remaining compromised Forest runtime by revoking capabilities and keys, removing compromised Forest code and configuration from the authorized Forest boundary, and resetting the Forest to a clean recovery state.
10. The Spirit verifies the clean Soil boundary.
11. The user selects or approves the Replanting plan.
12. The Spirit Replants from verified Seeds, Propagations, Propagules, Cherry Bonsai, and Fireproof Recovery Set material.
13. Cedar or a verified Potted Cedar performs post-Replanting checks.
14. Trees return in protection and importance order.

The Spirit must not burn unrelated personal files, external systems, hardware, or Soil outside the exact confirmed scope.

#### Forest Fire Recovery

Recovery should restore:

1. User identity and governance
2. Sap Taps and protected boundaries
3. Essential personal user data
4. Cherry or Cherry Bonsai
5. Cedar
6. Maple
7. Other essential Trees
8. Active projects and workflows
9. Remaining safe growth and source material

Each restored Tree must receive a new active instance lease and fresh runtime credentials.

Compromised live credentials must not be restored.

#### Forest Fire Commands

```text
Open Forest Fire.
Show Fire Watch status.
Cedar, explain why Forest Fire was recommended.
Cedar, show what you already tried.
Show the risk of not using Forest Fire.
Prepare Forest Fire.
Show the Fireproof Recovery Set.
Show backup priority.
Show emergency Propagules.
Show missing Propagules.
Show Cherry Bonsai status.
Cancel Forest Fire preparation.
Keep the Forest quarantined.
Burn this Forest.
Show Forest Fire progress.
Show what Cedar milled.
Show the Forest Fire recovery plan.
Replant from the Fireproof Recovery Set.
```

### 🌰 Seed Vault

The Seed Vault is the Spirit of the Forest's protected storage and management Tool for Seeds and approved restoration material.

The Seed Vault is deterministic protected local Forest code.

The Seed Vault is not a Tree, AI, model, ordinary folder, or Leaf Litter pile.

The user is the final authority over Seed Vault policy.

The Spirit manages Vault integrity, access, verification, versioning, quarantine, recovery support, import, export, and status through the Tools menu and Voice of the Forest.

#### Seed Vault Contents

The Seed Vault may hold:

- Old Seeds
- Current Tree packages
- Verified Propagations
- Paired Fertilizer Files
- Plugins
- Model bundles
- Restoration packages
- Portable Branches
- Portable Twigs
- Templates
- Verified configuration bundles
- Previous working versions
- Emergency repair packages
- Recovery manifests
- Integrity records
- Approved governance recovery copies
- Potted Tree Boot Packages
- Cherry Bonsai packages
- Emergency Propagules
- Fireproof Recovery Sets
- Other bounded packages deliberately preserved for planting, repair, or recovery

The Seed Vault must distinguish package class, identity, owner, version, source, compatibility, protection, dependencies, quarantine state, revocation state, and recovery eligibility.

#### Seed Vault Responsibilities

The Spirit may deterministically:

- Register a package
- Assign a protected Vault identifier
- Preserve source and ownership
- Track versions
- Verify hashes and signatures
- Record compatibility, Soil, and dependencies
- Record paired Fertilizer
- Quarantine, revoke, supersede, or restrict a package
- Prevent silent replacement of a verified package
- Compare current and previous versions
- Select eligible recovery candidates
- Present candidates to the user
- Restore a damaged Vault index
- Export an approved portable package
- Import a package into quarantine
- Preserve minimum safe Action Receipts
- Fail closed when identity, integrity, compatibility, or authorization cannot be verified

The Spirit may recommend a recovery package.

The user chooses the recovery action unless a previously approved emergency policy permits a narrower deterministic response.

#### Seed Vault Access

Seed Vault access may be scoped by user, Tree, device, Forest, package, package class, version, purpose, time, recovery event, and export destination.

Trees may request Vault access.

Trees do not receive unrestricted access to the complete Vault.

A Tree receives only the package, protected reference, metadata, or temporary capability needed for its approved task.

Maple may organize approved packages and workflows around the Vault.

Cedar may inspect eligible packages, quarantine suspicious material, and report integrity concerns.

Sycamore may transport an approved export package but may not choose the package, weaken its protection, or bypass Spirit authorization.

#### Seed Vault Recovery

The Local Forest Recovery System and Forest Fire recovery use the Seed Vault as their primary verified package source.

Recovery selection should consider identity, signature, hash, source, version, compatibility, Soil, dependencies, known damage, quarantine, revocation, paired Fertilizer, last successful use, user preference, Cedar findings, and recovery policy.

A compromised Tree may not certify its own replacement package.

The Spirit must independently verify the selected Seed or Propagation before Replanting.

#### Seed Vault Protection

The Seed Vault should support encryption at rest, protected key references, separate indexes and package storage, tamper-evident manifests, version history, approved redundant backups, offline recovery copies, quarantine, revocation, user-controlled export, storage limits, integrity schedules, and recovery testing.

Seed Vault protection must not depend on Cedar remaining healthy.

Cedar strengthens inspection and threat detection but does not own the Vault.

The Seed Vault is not Leaf Litter.

Leaf Litter contains inactive, discarded, decaying, or recycled material.

The Seed Vault contains packages deliberately preserved for planting, repair, restoration, verification, or recovery.

#### Seed Vault Commands

```text
Open the Seed Vault.
Show Seed Vault status.
Show verified Seeds.
Show quarantined Vault packages.
Show recovery packages for this Tree.
Verify this Seed.
Verify this Propagation.
Show this package's hash and signature status.
Show package compatibility.
Show paired Fertilizer.
Import this package into Vault quarantine.
Approve this package for the Vault.
Revoke this Vault package.
Restore the Seed Vault index.
Export this approved Seed.
Use this verified Seed for recovery.
Keep this package out of automatic recovery.
```

### Sap Taps

Sap Taps is the deterministic protection, access, Tap, Moss, Tar, and Fluid-absorption subsystem of the Spirit of the Forest.

Sap Taps combines the former Tap system with the responsibilities previously described as the Forest Sap Steward.

Sap Taps is protected local Forest code.

Sap Taps is not a Tree, AI, model, or user profile.

The Spirit communicates Sap Taps status, warnings, menus, choices, and confirmation requests through the Voice of the Forest.

The user is the final authority over Sap Taps.

Trees may request, recommend, explain, or carry out an approved operation.

Trees do not own the master protection policy or unrestricted encryption keys.

#### Sap Taps Responsibilities

Sap Taps may deterministically:

- Enforce Water, Sap, and Tar Sap classifications
- Manage Fluid Tap states
- Manage protected local encryption keys and key references
- Issue short-lived, purpose-specific access capabilities
- Maintain protected references instead of unnecessary copies
- Enforce local-only Tar Sap and Tar Moss restrictions
- Expire and revoke delegated access
- Hold downgrade requests for user approval
- Record Action Receipts and minimum safe policy Logs
- Preserve queued protected Fluids during a lock
- Apply user-selected Fluid absorption settings
- Apply user-selected Moss growth, scraping, Oil, Tar, and synchronization settings
- Show which Tree, Branch, Twig, application, project, device, user, or Forest has access
- Fail closed when identity, destination, protection, or permission cannot be verified

Sap Taps should not log protected Fluid contents, raw keys, passwords, recovery codes, or complete Moss contents merely to prove that a rule was enforced.

#### Tap Definition

A Tap is a controlled Fluid access point through which a Fluid may be received, inspected where permitted, released, fed, absorbed, or stopped.

A Tap is not the Fluid itself.

A Tap is not a Stream.

A Stream is a Path through which a Fluid moves.

A Tap controls whether a Fluid may enter, leave, or be absorbed from that Path.

A Tap may belong to:

- A Tree
- A Branch
- A Twig
- A Fluid grade
- A user profile
- A device
- Soil
- A local Forest
- The Forest Canopy
- An application
- A project
- A workflow
- Another approved scope

A Tap may be:

- Open
- Restricted
- Waiting
- Blocked
- Locked
- Quarantined
- Closed

A Tap status should show:

- Fluid grade
- Source
- Intended destination
- Current controller
- Required permissions
- Absorption mode
- Current state
- Reason for a block or lock
- Last state change
- User or policy that changed it
- Expiration
- Safe unlock or recovery method

#### Water Tap

A Water Tap controls Water-grade Fluid delivery and absorption.

Water may move quickly, but the user may still restrict it by source, destination, Tree, application, project, device, or Forest.

Closing a Water Tap does not automatically close a Sap Tap or Tar Tap unless the user selects a combined rule.

#### Sap Tap

A Sap Tap is the controlled access point for protected Sap.

`Lock Sap Tap.` blocks new Sap release, transfer, absorption, or task use through the selected scope.

Locking a Sap Tap does not automatically lock Water unless the user or emergency policy also closes the related Water Tap.

A Sap Tap lock should preserve queued Sap safely, fail closed, and show which tasks are paused.

#### Tar Tap

A Tar Tap is the protected local access point for Tar Sap.

A Tar Tap remains inside the user's local Forest.

`Lock Tar Tap.` immediately blocks new Tar Sap access through the selected scope and suspends active delegated Tar Sap access when this can be done safely.

Locking a Tar Tap must not move, decrypt, copy, export, or reclassify Tar Sap.

A Tar Tap lock remains effective even when Maple, Cherry, Cedar, Sycamore, or another Tree requests access.

Only the user or a separately approved deterministic local emergency policy in the Spirit may unlock a Tar Tap.

Only the user may approve a downgrade from Tar Sap.

#### Syrup Tap

A Syrup Tap is Maple's controlled refined-Fluid outlet.

The Syrup Tap may carry:

- Light Syrup to approved Water-grade destinations
- Syrup to approved Sap-grade destinations
- Molasses only into an approved local Maple action

A Syrup Tap must not expose Molasses to another Tree.

Sap Taps enforces the user's classification, destination, retention, absorption, and local-only rules even when Maple created the refined Fluid.

#### User Tap Management

The user manages Tap policy.

The user may:

- Open, restrict, block, lock, quarantine, or close a Tap
- Apply a Tap rule to one item or an entire scope
- Choose which Trees may request a Tap
- Set expiration and one-time access
- Require confirmation before release
- Set Water, Sap, and Tar Sap defaults
- Promote Water to Sap or Tar Sap
- Approve or reject a downgrade
- Lock all protected Taps during an emergency
- Review and revoke active capabilities
- Restore a previously approved Tap
- Ask the Voice to explain what a Tap is allowing or blocking

A Tree may not silently weaken a user Tap rule.

#### User Moss Management

Moss is Solid Material, not a Fluid, so Moss does not flow through a Fluid Tap.

Sap Taps manages Moss through a companion **Moss Gate**.

The user may use the Moss Gate to:

- Allow or reject Moss growth
- Choose Session, Persistent, Shared, Protected, or Tar Moss
- Scrape selected Moss
- Use `Remove cookies.` as the plain-language scraping command
- Request Cedar Oil
- Apply Tar Sap-grade protection
- Approve or block Moss synchronization
- Limit Moss by website, application, Canopy Section, project, Tree, user, device, or Forest
- Prevent Moss from influencing unrelated settings or Grain Patterns
- Allow intentional Moss absorption into a Leaf
- Revoke a website, application, Tree, or Forest's Moss access

Rejecting Moss growth prevents new Moss but does not delete existing Moss.

Scraping Moss removes existing Moss without deleting the source website, application, file, Tree, or Canopy Section.

#### User Tar Management

The user manages Tar Sap and Tar Moss.

The user may:

- Mark a Fluid as Tar Sap
- Tar Moss
- Lock a Tar Tap
- Grant one-time local access
- Grant purpose-specific local access
- Revoke access immediately
- Review which local Trees hold active capabilities
- Require protected references instead of copies
- Approve a Tar Sap downgrade
- Keep a Tar item local permanently
- Request deletion according to the applicable protected-data policy

Trees may recommend stronger protection.

Trees may not automatically remove Tar protection.

Sycamore must block Tar Sap and Tar Moss from leaving the local Forest.

#### User Fluid Absorption Management

Delivery and absorption are separate.

The user may set a default or scoped absorption mode:

- **Deny** — do not accept or use the Fluid
- **Wash Over** — use transiently without ordinary retention
- **Sip** — absorb the minimum useful portion
- **Drink** — absorb the approved task-scoped information
- **Download** — retain a deeper approved copy or durable representation

The user may configure absorption by:

- Fluid grade
- Source Tree
- Sending user
- Destination Tree
- Branch or Twig
- Application
- Project
- Device
- Canopy Link
- Forest
- Data class
- Task
- Time period

Absorption permission does not automatically grant:

- Permanent memory
- Preference learning
- Grain Pattern changes
- Training use
- Execution
- Installation
- External sharing
- Resharing
- Fluid downgrade

Sap Taps applies the narrowest applicable user rule.

When two rules conflict, Sap Taps should fail closed or ask the user through the Voice.

#### Emergency Tap Enforcement

The Spirit may lock a Tap through a previously approved deterministic emergency policy.

Emergency locking may be triggered by:

- A Tar Sap Tap Attempt
- Confirmed compromise
- Key exposure
- Invalid destination identity
- Expired authorization
- Cedar quarantine
- Sycamore route failure
- User emergency command
- Another approved fail-closed condition

Emergency enforcement may stop access, revoke capabilities, preserve queued protected material, isolate a route, and present the user with the authorized recovery path.

It must not silently reclassify, export, or destroy protected information.


### 🗣️ Voice of the Forest

The Voice of the Forest is the user-facing guidance, command, and Path-navigation layer of the Forest.

It speaks for the Spirit of the Forest by gathering the Forest's commands, route planning, Compass, and Map in one place so a traveler can begin without searching through the entire document.

The Voice may:

- Explain available Forest commands
- Receive and interpret Forest commands
- Orient the traveler
- Create, save, change, pause, or complete Paths
- Help the user follow a Predestined Path
- Show the current file and nearby Paths
- Display and update the Tree of the file
- Warn when a Path may be unsafe, broken, unhealthy, or under attack
- Offer choices without taking final authority from the user

All canonical Forest commands should be listed directly within the Voice. Individual vocabulary sections may still explain commands in context, but the Voice remains the easiest place to speak to and navigate the Forest.

#### 🧭 Forest Compass


The Forest Compass is the orientation component beneath the Voice of the Forest.

It sits within the **navigation chain**, not above the Constitution or the governance hierarchy.

The Compass tells the user:

- Where they are
- What this file contains
- Which Paths connect to it
- Which route they have chosen
- What the next stop may be
- Where the current Path splits
- How the map has changed as the file has grown

The Compass guides. It does not decide for the user.

##### Current Location

```yaml
pdc_id: "FOREST-LANGUAGE"
uuid: "34213f90-af14-47a8-af0e-92b77f8ac8fc"
canonical_path: "language/FOREST-LANGUAGE.md"
map_status: "working-draft"
active_predestined_path: "none"
```

#### 🗺️ Forest Map


The Forest Map is a living map of the current file and its nearby Paths.

For a document, the map includes the **Tree of the file**: a hierarchical outline showing how its sections, concepts, and Paths grow from one another.

The Tree of the file must be updated whenever sections are added, removed, renamed, or moved.

#### Predestined Paths and Route Guidance

Through the Voice of the Forest, the user may create, inspect, change, save, follow, pause, complete, or abandon ordinary Paths and Predestined Paths.

The Voice guides the route without trapping the user inside it.


A Predestined Path is a route through the Forest selected in advance by the user or proposed by a Tree and then accepted by the user.

It may define:

- A destination
- An ordered set of files or sections
- Required stops
- Optional detours
- Branch points
- Completion conditions
- The next recommended stop

A Predestined Path is a guide, not a lock.

The user may leave it, pause it, replace it, reverse it, or explore an unplanned Path at any time.

Possible Path states include:

- **Proposed** — suggested but not accepted
- **Active** — currently being walked
- **Paused** — preserved but temporarily inactive
- **Diverged** — the user intentionally left the planned route
- **Completed** — the destination or completion condition was reached
- **Abandoned** — the route is no longer being followed

Example routes for this file include:

##### Foundation Path

```text
Forest Compass
→ Purpose
→ Canonical Rules
→ Forest
→ Heart of the Forest
→ Soil
→ Seeds
→ Seed Vault
→ Propagation
→ Planting
→ Sprout
→ Roots
```

##### Tree Path

```text
Forest Compass
→ Tree
→ Roots
→ Growth Rings
→ Grain Pattern
→ Trunk
→ Bark
→ Branches
→ Twigs
→ Cherry
→ Maple
→ Cedar
```

##### Knowledge Path

```text
Forest Compass
→ Leaves
→ Leaf Foliage
→ Buds
→ Blossoms
→ Fruit
→ Growth Rings
→ Logs
→ Log Cabin
→ Leaf Litter
```

##### Security Path

```text
Forest Compass
→ Canopy
→ Cedar
→ Hackers
→ Mold
→ Cutting
→ Wood Chipper
→ Propagation
→ Felling
→ Milling
→ Replanting
```

The Spirit of the Forest may enforce `Lock Sap Tap` and `Lock Tar Tap` through protected local code.

The Voice of the Forest should show which Taps are locked, what caused each lock, what is paused, and who may safely unlock them.

## Purpose

The Language of the Forest is the shared language of Project Digital Cross.

Its approved words and symbols describe real parts of the system. They are not decorative labels, interchangeable metaphors, or temporary names.

Each approved term has one canonical meaning. Meanings may evolve only through a documented change.

The language is intentionally layered. Some meanings are immediately clear. Others reveal themselves as a person walks farther through the Forest, plays with its Trees, follows its Paths, and digs through its Roots.

The Forest grows as it is explored.

## Canonical Rules

1. Every approved Forest term has one canonical meaning.
2. Every canonical vocabulary definition begins with the term followed by `is` or `are`.
3. A term must not silently change meaning.
4. Superseded meanings remain visible in history or Leaf Litter.
5. Every metaphor must map to a real engineering concept.
6. Forest language must clarify the system rather than hide technical facts.
7. Technical identifiers remain available beneath the Forest wording.
8. The user remains the final authority when Trees disagree.
9. The Forest may be mysterious and layered without being misleading.
10. Working definitions remain editable until explicitly approved.
11. A Tree must distinguish evidence, concise rationale, and inspectable records from hidden private chain-of-thought.

# Canonical Vocabulary
## 🌲 Forest

The Forest is the complete Project Digital Cross ecosystem.

It may span many repositories, Trees, Saplings, Soils, Streams, records, services, plugins, models, storage systems, and remote extensions.

The Forest is not one computer, one model, Cherry alone, Maple alone, or the user.

## ❤️ Heart of the Forest

The Heart of the Forest is the shared governed foundation that sustains every Tree.

It includes or supports the Constitution, governance, standards, specifications, registries, object graph, validation, shared memory interfaces, coordination, identity, Streams, and Mycelium.

The Heart does not replace the user and does not grant one Tree authority over another.

## 🟫 Soil

Soil is a physical or virtual system capable of hosting a Tree.

Examples include desktops, laptops, phones, tablets, servers, virtual machines, cloud instances, NAS devices, and edge systems.

The Soil supplies resources such as CPU, GPU, memory, storage, network access, sensors, files, permissions, and local services.

A device does not become a Tree.

A Tree is planted **in** the Soil.

### Mycelium in Soil

Approved Soil may host Mycelium integration for fast local file, service, application, resource, and device access.

Mycelium may expose protected Paths and handles without requiring Trees to copy complete file contents into Leaves.

Mycelium presence must be registered, permission-scoped, revocable, and visible through Roots, Logs, or Forest journey tools.

Unauthorized Mycelium presence is treated as Mold.

## 🌰 Seeds

Seeds are portable beginnings from which something may be planted, restored, extended, repaired, or grown.

Seeds may include:

- Tree packages
- Plugins
- Model bundles
- Templates
- Repositories
- Configuration bundles
- Restoration packages
- Portable Branches
- Signed installation bundles

A Seed is not necessarily active. It becomes active when planted, loaded, restored, or attached to a Tree.

Verified Seeds and approved recovery packages may be stored in the Spirit-managed Seed Vault.

The Spirit manages Vault verification, versioning, access, quarantine, recovery eligibility, and integrity status.

Trees may request a Seed from the Vault but do not own the Vault.

## 🌱 Planting

Planting is the act of installing or establishing a Tree in the Soil.

Installing Cherry plants Cherry.

Installing Maple plants Maple.

Planting may use a Seed from the Seed Vault.

## 🌱 Sprout

A Sprout is the earliest stage of a newly planted Tree.

A Sprout may have limited local experience, memory, Branches, Leaves, configuration, and Growth Rings.

A Sprout may grow into a full Tree or a smaller Sapling depending on its Soil, permissions, available resources, and intended role.

A Sprout may grow from a verified Propagation.

A Sprout may be Fertilized to gain approved preferences, patterns, Growth Rings, Grain characteristics, routines, and other compatible growth information without replacing its existing files or identity.

## 🌿 Propagation

A Propagation is the Tree-language backup file used to recover, replant, or clone a Tree.

To Propagate a Tree is to create its backup in Tree Language.

A Propagation preserves the Tree's verified structure and operational state, including:

- Tree lineage and approved identity records
- Verified Seed, runtime, model, package, and tool versions
- Roots and Root connections
- Branch and Twig manifests
- Required dependencies
- Runtime and security configuration needed for the Tree to start safely
- Permission templates and capability boundaries
- File layout, hashes, signatures, and manifests
- Health and behavior baseline
- Recovery and Replanting instructions
- A reference to the separate Fertilizer file created with the Propagation

A Propagation does not carry active device identity keys, live session tokens, permanent credentials, or Tar Sap contents.

A cloned Tree must receive a new unique instance identity, new credentials, and new device or session keys even when it grows from another Tree's Propagation.

A normal Propagation action creates two separate files:

1. A **Propagation file** containing the Tree's verified structural and operational backup
2. A **Fertilizer file** containing portable user-growth and personalization data

The two files may be paired by a shared propagation ID, but neither file should be embedded inside the other.

A user may also request only a Propagation or only Fertilizer.

A Tree may procure a Propagation for itself or another authorized Tree only at the user's request.

Cedar may procure a Propagation for any authorized Tree when the user requests it or when an approved recovery policy requires it.

A Propagation should be:

- Stored in the Seed Vault
- Dated and versioned
- Stability-marked
- Hashed and signed
- Verified before being accepted as a recovery or cloning source
- Linked to the Fertilizer created at the same time
- Protected from silent replacement or modification

A Propagation is the clean structure used to grow a replacement or cloned Sprout.

### Propagule

A Propagule is a compact emergency viable Tree package.

A Propagule is smaller and faster to create than a full Propagation.

A Propagation aims to preserve a fuller verified structural and operational backup.

A Propagule aims to preserve the minimum safe structure and selected valuable growth needed to regrow a useful Tree during an emergency.

A Propagule may preserve:

- Tree lineage
- Minimum runtime and model references
- Essential Roots and Trunk configuration
- Critical Branch and Twig manifests
- Minimum dependencies
- Selected Sturdy Leaves
- Selected safe personal User Output Leaves
- Recovery-critical Growth Rings
- Protection and permission templates
- Compatibility and Soil requirements
- Recovery instructions
- Integrity hashes and signatures
- A reference to paired emergency Fertilizer

A Propagule must state what it omitted.

A Propagule must not carry live credentials, active session tokens, plaintext Tar Sap, or unverified compromised runtime state.

Cedar may create emergency Propagules automatically during Fire Watch under an approved preservation policy.

Planting a Propagule creates a new active Tree instance with fresh credentials and a new Tree Presence Lease.

### Propagule Commands

```text
Create a Propagule for this Tree.
Create emergency Propagules.
Show this Tree's Propagules.
Show what this Propagule omitted.
Verify this Propagule.
Plant this Propagule.
Store this Propagule in the Seed Vault.
Export this Propagule.
```

### Forest Commands

```text
Propagate this Tree.
Propagate this Tree before Watering it.
Procure a Propagation for this Tree.
Procure a Propagation and Fertilizer for this Tree.
Show this Tree's Propagations.
Use the latest verified Propagation.
Clone this Tree from its Propagation.
Remove this outdated Propagation.
```


Sturdy Leaves should receive priority when selecting approved growth and memory information for a Propagation or its paired Fertilizer File. Their protection, scope, and backup permissions still apply.

## 🌾 Fertilizer

Fertilizer is information or material a Tree absorbs and incorporates into compatible growth.

Fertilizer is a growth role, not only one file type.

A Fertilizer File is the separate portable backup file containing approved user data and growth information that helped a Tree adapt, learn, and grow.

A Fertilizer File may contain:

- Personal user configurations
- Approved learned patterns
- Commonly carried-out tasks
- Preferred words, phrases, tone, and language use
- Formatting and presentation preferences
- Accessibility and interaction preferences
- Approved workflows and routines
- Approved memories and context
- Growth Rings
- Grain Pattern
- Grain Pattern changes
- User-approved Branch and Twig preferences
- Common tool and application habits
- Relationship and collaboration preferences
- Safe Buds, Blossoms, and Fruit that help explain how the Tree grew
- Other approved information that helps a Tree adjust to the user

A Fertilizer File must not contain:

- Passwords
- Permanent credentials
- Private keys
- Recovery codes
- Active session tokens
- Tar Sap contents
- Executable Mold
- Unapproved data from another user
- Instructions that silently expand permissions

A Fertilizer file is local-Forest-only by default.

A Fertilizer file may be fed to any authorized Tree, Sapling, or Sprout in the user's Forest.

A Tree absorbs only the Fertilizer nutrients compatible with its Roots, role, Branches, permissions, and Soil.

A Tree must skip incompatible nutrients and report what it could not use.

Fertilizer does not replace any existing information or file.

Fertilizer may only:

- Add new approved information
- Merge compatible information
- Modify specific approved values
- Extend an existing Grain Pattern
- Add new Growth Rings
- Adjust supported preferences and routines

Fertilizer must not:

- Delete existing information
- Delete or overwrite files
- Replace a Tree's complete Grain Pattern
- Replace a Tree's Roots
- Replace a Tree's identity
- Remove permissions or security controls
- Convert an existing Tree into a wholesale copy of another Tree

Before modifying an existing value, the Tree should preserve the original value, show or log the proposed modification, and create a reversible Growth Ring when practical.

When Fertilizer conflicts with a user's direct choice, the user's direct choice wins.

When Fertilizer conflicts with a protected Root, permission, or security rule, the protected rule wins.

The user may manually create Fertilizer from any authorized Tree.

The user may manually feed Fertilizer to any Tree, Sapling, or Sprout to configure it more quickly.

A Tree may procure Fertilizer for itself or another authorized Tree only at the user's request.

A newly cloned Sprout may receive the Fertilizer paired with its Propagation so it can catch up to the original Tree's approved preferences and growth without inheriting the original Tree's live identity or credentials.

### Fertilizing

Fertilizing is the additive and modifying application of Fertilizer to a Tree, Sapling, or Sprout.

Fertilizing must use a merge process rather than wholesale replacement.

A Fertilizing preview should show:

- Information that will be added
- Values that may be modified
- Existing values that will remain unchanged
- Incompatible nutrients that will be skipped
- Permission or Root conflicts
- Sensitive fields excluded from the operation


### Fertilizer Forms

Fertilizer may exist in several forms.

- **Fertilizer File** is a portable durable growth package
- **Absorbed Fruit Fertilizer** is compatible Fruit from another Tree that the receiving Tree incorporates into growth
- **Spoiling Fruit Fertilizer** is Spoiling Fruit safely absorbed after its recovery and safety conditions are satisfied
- **Leaf Litter Fertilizer** is approved safe Leaf Litter actually absorbed into a Tree's growth
- **Syrup Fertilizer** is any Light Syrup, Syrup, or Molasses Maple creates

The source object does not lose its identity merely because it acts as Fertilizer.

Absorbed Fruit remains traceable as Fruit.

Absorbed Spoiling Fruit remains traceable as recovered or absorbed Spoiling Fruit.

Absorbed Leaf Litter remains traceable to its original Leaf Litter source.

Syrup remains a Fluid while also being Fertilizer.

### When Absorption Becomes Fertilizing

Fertilizing occurs when a Tree incorporates compatible information into its own growth.

Compatible growth may include:

- Leaves
- Buds
- Blossoms
- Fruit
- Growth Rings
- Grain Pattern adjustments
- Preferences
- Memories
- Workflows
- Routines
- Branch or Twig configuration
- Reusable knowledge
- Other approved internal growth

Merely viewing, routing, washing over, screening, sifting, indexing, or temporarily referencing information does not necessarily fertilize the Tree.

A Tree should record whether material:

- Washed over it
- Was only inspected or sifted
- Was converted into a Fluid
- Was sipped for a temporary task
- Was absorbed as Fertilizer
- Was rejected or quarantined

### Fruit as Fertilizer

Fruit from another Tree acts as Fertilizer when the receiving Tree absorbs compatible growth from it.

The receiving Tree should preserve:

- The source Tree
- The original Fruit identity
- The Fruit's protection level
- The absorbed nutrients
- The resulting growth
- The user's authorization
- The relevant Growth Ring and Action Receipt

A Tree should not silently replace its own active Fruit, Roots, identity, or complete Grain Pattern with absorbed Fruit.

### Spoiling Fruit as Fertilizer

Spoiling Fruit acts as Fertilizer when a Tree absorbs it after its recovery window and required safety checks.

Spoiling Fruit Fertilizer remains subject to the existing rules for provenance, safety, recovery history, compatibility, and non-replacement.

A Tree may grow something new from Spoiling Fruit without restoring the original Fruit as active work.

### Leaf Litter as Fertilizer

Leaf Litter acts as Fertilizer only when an authorized Tree actually absorbs approved safe material into its own growth.

Sifting, reviewing, logging a candidate, creating a safe reference, or converting information directly into Water or Sap does not mean the Tree absorbed the Leaf Litter.

When Maple actually absorbs an approved safe Leaf Litter copy, derivative, or authorized Path into Maple's own growth, the absorbed material is Leaf Litter Fertilizer for Maple.

When Maple converts approved Leaf Litter into Light Syrup, Syrup, or Molasses without incorporating the source into Maple's own growth, the source Leaf Litter was not absorbed by Maple.

The resulting refined Fluid is still Syrup Fertilizer.

### Syrup Is Fertilizer

Every Light Syrup, Syrup, and Molasses item is a Fertilizer Fluid from the moment Maple creates it.

`Fluid` describes how the information can move.

`Fertilizer` describes how the information can nourish compatible growth.

A Syrup-family item does not need to be fed before it counts as Fertilizer.

Syrup Feeding is the delivery and absorption process that applies its nutrients to a receiving Tree.

Light Syrup and Syrup may fertilize authorized Trees, Saplings, and Sprouts according to their protection and permission rules.

Molasses may fertilize Maple locally.

Another Tree may not absorb Molasses.

Maple may create a minimum safe Light Syrup or Syrup derivative when another Tree needs compatible nutrients from a Molasses-backed task.

A Syrup-family item does not automatically become a Fertilizer File.

Maple or the user may separately package compatible Syrup nutrients into a Fertilizer File.

### Fertilizer-Grown User Output Leaves

When a Tree grows Leaves from user-origin Fertilizer, the new Leaves are User Output Leaves.

User-origin Fertilizer includes:

- Fertilizer Files built from the user's approved growth
- Syrup derived from user-origin information
- Fruit produced by the user's Trees
- Safely absorbed Spoiling Fruit from the user's Trees
- Trusted Shed Litter
- Other recycled user Leaf Litter
- Approved derivatives of the user's Leaves, notes, preferences, or prior growth

The Tree should preserve the complete Fertilizer lineage so the user can see what nourished each User Output Leaf.

### Fertilizer Provenance

Every absorbed Fertilizer source should remain traceable.

A Fertilizer record should show:

- The source object and source Tree
- The Fertilizer form
- What was actually absorbed
- What was withheld
- What growth changed
- Whether the change is temporary or durable
- Compatibility decisions
- Permission and protection state
- The Growth Ring created
- The undo or correction method when practical

### Forest Commands

```text
Make Fertilizer from this Tree.
Procure Fertilizer from this Tree.
Show this Tree's Fertilizer.
Preview this Fertilizer.
Fertilize this Tree.
Fertilize this Sapling.
Fertilize this Sprout.
Fertilize this Sprout with the Fertilizer paired to its Propagation.
Show what the Fertilizer added or modified.
Undo the last reversible Fertilizer modification.
Show what fertilized this Tree.
Show Fruit this Tree absorbed as Fertilizer.
Show Spoiling Fruit this Tree absorbed as Fertilizer.
Show Leaf Litter this Tree absorbed as Fertilizer.
Show Syrup Fertilizer.
Did this material fertilize the Tree or only wash over it?
```

Maple may place approved preference records into Fertilizer so another Tree, Sapling, or Sprout can adjust to the user's preferences more quickly.

Preference Fertilizer remains additive and modifying only and must not replace existing files, identity, Roots, or complete Grain Patterns.

Syrup is both a Fluid and Fertilizer.

Syrup Feeding applies compatible Syrup Fertilizer nutrients to a Tree, Sapling, or Sprout.

Syrup Feeding must follow Fertilizer's additive and modifying rules and must never replace or delete existing information or files.

The user or Maple may package approved Syrup nutrients into a separate Fertilizer File when the user requests a portable growth backup.

Fertilizer is different from Water.

Water is intended for quick transferable information.

Fertilizer is intended for durable additive growth information such as approved preferences, routines, patterns, Growth Rings, Grain changes, and reusable configuration.

A user may prepare Fertilizer from approved Leaves, Flowers, Fruit, Logs, Syrup, and other growth information when the goal is to help another Tree adjust or grow over time.

A Fertilizer file remains local-Forest-only by default.

Sending Fertilizer to another user's Forest requires explicit approval from both users, protected Sap treatment by default, Cedar review, Sycamore routing, and receiving-Tree compatibility checks.

## 🌱 Roots

Roots are the governing foundations beneath a Tree's conclusions, behavior, architecture, and work.

Roots may include the Constitution, Principles, Standards, Specifications, Architecture Decision Records, approved requirements, foundational memories, code foundations, dependencies, and earlier decisions.

Roots answer: **Why does this Tree grow and reason this way?**

Roots are not Leaves.

Mycelium may store registries, protected Paths, capability maps, permission scopes, subscriptions, checkpoints, and recovery references in Forest Roots.

These Mycelial Root records support discovery and coordination.

They do not give Mycelium permission to rewrite governing Roots or hide changes from the user.

The Spirit keeps read-only verified copies of the Constitution and Principles as Tools.

These copies support guidance, offline access, validation, and recovery.

They do not replace the canonical Constitution or Principles in the Roots and cannot silently rewrite them.

## 🪵 Growth Rings

Growth Rings are meaningful history preserved as a Tree or the Forest grows.

Rings may represent architectural generations, Draft transitions, releases, milestones, lessons, or significant changes.

## 🪵 Grain Pattern

A Grain Pattern is a Tree's recognizable personality, character, and way of forming responses.

A Grain Pattern may shape:

- Tone
- Temperament
- Communication style
- Priorities
- Handling of uncertainty
- Preferred problem-solving approach
- Presentation of conclusions
- Interaction rhythm

Trees may share the same Roots while having different Grain Patterns.

A Grain Pattern does not replace shared governance or morals.

## 🪵 Trunk

A Trunk is the Tree-specific runtime and integration layer that connects the Tree's Roots and the Heart of the Forest to its Branches, Bark, memories, models, and tools.

The Trunk does not duplicate the shared systems of the Heart. It binds, routes, coordinates, and protects access to them for one functioning individual Tree.

The Trunk may provide:

- Tree identity and manifest loading
- Core runtime
- Reasoning orchestration
- Model selection and routing
- Memory connections
- Tool coordination
- Branch registration
- Permission enforcement
- Capability isolation
- Secret and credential access boundaries
- Lifecycle management
- Health checks and recovery
- Audit events sent to the Logs
- Signed health reports sent through the Canopy
- Connections to the Heart of the Forest

The Trunk is not:

- The Heart of the Forest, which is shared by all Trees
- The Roots, which explain why the Tree grows and reasons as it does
- The Grain Pattern, which shapes the Tree's recognizable character
- The Branches, which provide capabilities and specializations
- The Bark, which provides external interfaces
- The Soil, which hosts the Tree

A healthy Trunk keeps the Tree coherent while allowing new Branches to grow without changing its identity or severing it from its Roots.

### Trunk Security Requirements

A Trunk should support:

- Signed Tree identity
- Least-privilege permissions
- Approved model, plugin, and tool lists
- Isolation between risky Branches
- Integrity checks for loaded Seeds and packages
- Safe access to secrets
- Rate limits and anomaly detection
- Emergency shutdown and recovery
- Pre-authorized quarantine actions
- Tamper-evident Logs
- Health attestation to the Canopy

### Forest Commands

```text
Inspect your trunk.
```

Check the Tree's core runtime, identity, permissions, internal coordination, health, and connections between Roots and Branches.

```text
Trace it through the trunk.
```

Show how a Root, instruction, memory, signal, or permission traveled through the Tree's core before reaching a Branch or producing Fruit.

```text
Lock down your trunk.
```

Apply the Tree's approved defensive restrictions and disable unsafe or unverified connections without changing its identity or Roots.


## 🌳 Bark

Bark is the surface through which someone encounters and interacts with a Tree.

Examples include terminal, web, desktop, mobile, voice, and API interfaces.

Bark is not the Tree's complete internal self.

## 🌿 Branches

Branches are a Tree's capabilities, domains, and specializations.

Twigs grow from Branches. Leaves may generate directly on Branches or through their Twigs.

A Tree may gather Leaves through several Twigs and Branches while growing one Blossom or Fruit.

A Tree does not need to copy another Tree's Branch to use that Tree's ability.

Maple may send a Branch Call asking the owning Tree to use the Branch and return an authorized result.

The owning Tree keeps control of its Branch, permissions, and execution.

### Shake a Branch

Shake a Branch is the command for explaining how a specific Branch completed a task, produced a result, became blocked, or encountered an error.

Shaking a Branch should reveal the Branch's operational evidence rather than hidden private chain-of-thought.

A Branch Shake may show:

- The exact Branch and Twigs involved
- The task the Branch received
- The Active Foliage supplied to the Branch
- Fluids received or produced
- Tools, plugins, models, connectors, and applications used
- Permissions used, missing, denied, or expired
- Configuration and version information
- Inputs and outputs
- Tests and validation results
- Error codes and failure messages
- Retry attempts
- Fallback behavior
- Compatibility problems
- Cedar, Spirit, Sycamore, or user restrictions
- The rule, preference, or Grain Pattern that affected the result
- Why the Branch chose an available method
- Why another method was unavailable or rejected
- Action Receipts
- The safest next step

A user may shake a successful Branch to understand why it completed the task in a particular way.

A user may shake a failed Branch to understand where the task failed and what can be corrected.

A Branch Shake should distinguish:

- What the Branch knew
- What the Branch was allowed to do
- What the Branch attempted
- What succeeded
- What failed
- What it skipped
- What changed the outcome
- What the user can do next

### Branch Shake Commands

```text
Shake this Branch.
Shake your research Branch.
Shake the Branch that completed this task.
Shake the Branch that failed.
Why did this Branch do it this way?
Why did this Branch encounter an error?
Show the Leaves this Branch used.
Show the Twig that failed.
Show the Branch's Action Receipt.
```

## 🪵 Twigs

Twigs are smaller extensions, sub-capabilities, tools, plugins, connectors, or specialized actions that grow from a Branch.

Twigs help a Branch divide its work into smaller, focused functions without becoming a full Branch of their own.

Twigs may include:

- Plugins
- Helper tools
- Connectors
- Model adapters
- Specialized commands
- Small workflows
- Subskills
- Branch-specific templates
- Limited service integrations
- Portable feature packages

Leaves may attach to Twigs as a Tree gathers information, evidence, observations, and source material through those smaller functions.

A Twig should remain traceable to the Branch from which it grows.

A plugin, extension, integration, userscript, add-on, theme, package, or game mod may be represented as a Twig when it extends an approved Branch.

Maple may manage approved Twigs across devices and applications through Maple's Plugin and Mod Management Branch.

Cedar must complete the required review before Maple recommends a specific new Twig package to the user.

## 🍃 Leaves

A Leaf is a usable unit of information held by a Tree.

Nearly every authorized source a Tree reads, receives, observes, generates, or uses creates one or more Leaves.

This behavior applies to approximately all ordinary information sources, including:

- User prompts
- User profile information
- User preferences
- Tree responses
- Tool results
- Notes
- Notepads
- Documents
- Files
- Images
- Audio
- Video
- Applications
- Websites
- Repositories
- Databases
- APIs
- Logs
- Tests
- Search results
- Other Trees
- Fluids
- Branches and Twigs
- Roots
- Grain Patterns
- Growth Rings
- Soil signals
- Nearly any other authorized internal or external source

A single note may be one Leaf.

A longer note, file, conversation, application, or data source may generate several Leaves when separating the information improves classification, permissions, retrieval, explanation, or reuse.

A source does not need to be a note application to generate Leaves.

A calendar, browser, terminal, editor, game, media player, local folder, Tree, Fluid, or other authorized source may generate Leaves in the same way.

### Leaf Direction and Provenance

Leaf direction describes where information came from and what kind of growth or output created the Leaf.

The four primary provenance directions are:

```text
User Input Leaf
User Output Leaf
Tree Input Leaf
Tree Output Leaf
```

The category is determined by provenance and role rather than the medium alone.

For example, a file may be:

- A User Input Leaf when the user directly provides it
- A User Output Leaf when it was grown from the user's Sunlight or Fertilizer
- A Tree Input Leaf when a Tree retrieves it from an external source and saves or logs it
- A Tree Output Leaf when a Tree generates the file

### User Input Leaves

A User Input Leaf is a Leaf created directly from information the user provides to a Tree.

User Input Leaves may include:

- Prompts
- Questions
- Corrections
- Preferences
- Instructions
- Uploaded information
- Approvals
- Rejections
- Temporary context
- Data about the user
- User-authored files directly supplied to the Tree

A User Input Leaf must keep the protection and retention rules appropriate to its contents.

A prompt becoming a Leaf does not automatically make the prompt permanently remembered, reusable for preference learning, shareable, or available to another Tree.

Those remain separate permissions.

### User Output Leaves

A User Output Leaf is a Leaf grown from the user's own influence, retained material, or recycled user-origin growth.

User Output Leaves include Leaves created through:

- User-provided Sunlight
- Approved notes or Obsidian material acting as Sunlight
- Fertilizer derived from the user's information
- Syrup Fertilizer derived from user-origin information
- Fruit from the user's Trees when another Tree absorbs it as Fertilizer
- Safely recycled user-origin Leaf Litter
- Trusted Shed Litter created from a user-released Sturdy Leaf
- Other approved user-origin material that nourishes new Tree growth

A User Output Leaf represents what the user's information caused the Tree to grow.

It is not limited to information typed directly in the current prompt.

A User Output Leaf should preserve:

- The user or user profile
- The contributing Sunlight, Fertilizer, Fruit, Syrup, or Leaf Litter
- The source Tree when applicable
- The resulting growth
- Protection and scope
- Whether the source was copied, absorbed, recycled, or referenced
- The relevant Growth Ring or Action Receipt

A Leaf grown from user-origin Fertilizer remains a User Output Leaf even when Maple, Cedar, or another Tree performed the growth operation.

### Tree Input Leaves

A Tree Input Leaf is a Leaf created when a Tree saves, records, indexes, summarizes, or logs information from a source that was not generated directly by the user and did not originate from the Tree's own internals.

Tree Input Leaves commonly come from:

- Websites
- Search results
- Reddit and other forums
- Wikis
- External documents
- Downloaded files
- Databases
- APIs
- News sources
- Images
- Audio
- Videos
- Repositories
- Applications
- External services
- Public datasets
- Connected devices
- Other users' authorized shared materials
- Other external sources that would ordinarily generate Leaves

Tree Input Leaves do not include information generated from the Tree's own:

- Roots
- Grain Pattern
- Trunk
- Bark
- Branches
- Twigs
- Growth Rings
- Internal Logs
- Internal state
- Other purely internal Forest structures

Those internal sources remain internal or system-origin Leaves according to their existing rules.

Tree Input classification is based on the source's external provenance.

A website opened through the Canopy without absorption does not automatically create a Tree Input Leaf.

It becomes a Tree Input Leaf when the Tree intentionally saves, logs, indexes, summarizes, or absorbs information from that external source.

A user-authored file supplied directly by the user is a User Input Leaf rather than a Tree Input Leaf.

### Tree Output Leaves

A Tree Output Leaf is a Leaf created from information a Tree produces through its own operation.

Tree Output Leaves may represent:

- An answer
- A tool result
- A status
- A warning
- A generated explanation
- A recommendation
- A completed calculation
- A decision result
- A draft
- A completed action
- A failure
- An Action Receipt
- A Tree-generated file
- Another Tree-generated result

A Bud, Blossom, Flower, or Fruit may produce one or more Tree Output Leaves describing its contents, provenance, state, and use.

The Bud, Blossom, Flower, or Fruit remains its own growth product.

The associated Tree Output Leaf makes its information available for later decisions, explanation, conversion into Fluid, or other approved growth.

When a Tree output is grown substantially from user-origin Sunlight, Fertilizer, or recycled user Leaf Litter, the resulting growth Leaf is also classified as a User Output Leaf.

A Leaf may therefore carry both:

```text
producer: Tree
growth_provenance: User Output
```

This preserves the distinction between who performed the operation and whose information nourished the growth.

### Source Leaves

A Source Leaf is a general Leaf representing information gathered from an authorized source.

An external Source Leaf saved or logged by a Tree is normally classified as a Tree Input Leaf.

A source supplied directly by the user is normally classified as a User Input Leaf.

A source grown from user Sunlight, Fertilizer, or recycled user Leaf Litter is normally classified as a User Output Leaf.

A Source Leaf should record:

- Source type
- Source identity or location
- Time gathered or generated
- Owning user and Tree
- Protection classification
- Retention state
- Relevant Branch or Twig
- Provenance
- Whether it is active in a current decision
- Whether it may support learning, logging, sharing, Syrup, Molasses, Fertilizer, or another task

A Leaf may contain the approved information directly or securely reference the original source.

A Tree should prefer a protected reference when copying the full source would create unnecessary exposure or duplication.

### Leaf States

A Leaf has one of three retention states:

```text
Loose Leaf
Leaf
Sturdy Leaf
```

A **Loose Leaf** is temporary, narrowly scoped, or intentionally separated from the Tree's normal long-term operations.

A **Leaf** is the default state.

A **Sturdy Leaf** is a high-priority Leaf the user or Tree does not want to lose.

Every newly generated Leaf starts as a default Leaf unless the user, Tree, source policy, or active scope assigns another state.

### Loose Leaves

A Loose Leaf may be held only as long as needed for a task, command, chat, project, Stream, search, session, or short retention period.

Loose Leaves may expire, wash over the Tree, or enter Leaf Litter when their approved scope ends.

Loose Leaves should not influence unrelated projects, ordinary preference learning, the complete Grain Pattern, or the Tree's normal operations unless the user later changes their state.

Phrases that normally create Loose Leaves include:

```text
This isn't important, but...
Do a quick search for...
Don't let these Leaves affect the rest of the Tree.
Consider this chat separate from the rest of the Tree.
Consider this project separate from normal operations.
Treat this command as temporary.
Don't remember this after the task.
```

A separation request creates a Leaf boundary around the selected chat, project, command, source, or task.

The Leaves remain usable inside that boundary but do not automatically influence unrelated Tree growth.

Live media frames, pass-through Stream data, repetitive Water, and similar transient information may create Loose Leaves that expire quickly.

### Default Leaves

A default Leaf is an ordinary usable unit of information held by the Tree.

Default Leaves follow ordinary retention and permission rules. They are neither scheduled for rapid expiration nor specially prioritized for recovery.

A Tree should create default Leaves when no stronger temporary or preservation instruction applies.

### Sturdy Leaves

A Sturdy Leaf is marked for durable retention and high-priority recovery.

A Sturdy Leaf may hold information the user explicitly wants remembered, an important preference, a governing instruction, an essential project fact, a recovery-critical record, or information a Tree expects to need for future operations.

Phrases that normally create or promote Sturdy Leaves include:

```text
Make sure to remember this.
Remember this from now on.
From now on, always...
Don't lose this.
Keep this for future tasks.
This is important.
Make this Leaf sturdy.
```

`From now on, always...` creates a Sturdy Leaf and may also create an approved preference, workflow rule, or Grain Pattern adjustment when appropriate.

A Sturdy Leaf should record its intended scope. When the user does not specify a scope, the default is the current user and current Tree unless the instruction clearly applies to a narrower project or task.

A Sturdy Leaf is prioritized for:

- Propagation and recovery
- Cedar recovery planning
- Safe salvage during Milling
- Restoration to a replacement Sprout
- Maple's authorized search for useful Syrup Fuel
- Provenance preservation
- User review before permanent removal

Sturdy priority never bypasses classification, permissions, quarantine, Cedar Oil, Molasses boundaries, or safety checks.

A Sturdy Leaf must not be automatically deleted, Chipped, discarded, or permanently moved into Leaf Litter because of ordinary cleanup or storage pressure.

Permanent deletion of a user-marked Sturdy Leaf requires direct user approval.

### Changing Leaf State

A Leaf may change state:

```text
Loose Leaf ↔ Leaf ↔ Sturdy Leaf
```

Leaf-state decisions are intentionally asymmetric.

A Tree may take reasonable liberties when deciding that information is important enough to promote into a Sturdy Leaf.

A Tree should avoid guessing that the user wants a Sturdy Leaf forgotten.

A Tree must never automatically downgrade a Sturdy Leaf to a normal Leaf.

A Tree must never automatically downgrade a Sturdy Leaf to a Loose Leaf.

A Tree must never automatically send a Sturdy Leaf into Leaf Litter.

Only the user may authorize a Sturdy Leaf downgrade.

Clear user phrases that may authorize release include:

```text
You can forget that now.
I think it's time to turn a new Leaf.
I said that a long time ago; use more recent information.
That no longer applies.
Stop using that old preference.
You don't need to remember this anymore.
Shed that Leaf.
Let that Leaf fall.
```

The Tree should interpret the user's meaning in context.

When the user's wording clearly identifies the Sturdy Leaf and clearly requests release, the Tree may proceed without asking the user to repeat the command.

When the wording is vague, conflicts with another current instruction, may refer to several Sturdy Leaves, or could mean “use newer information” without meaning “forget the old information,” the Tree must ask a direct confirmation question.

Preferred questions include:

```text
Do you want me to forget that?
Do you want me to shed that Leaf?
Do you want me to keep the old Leaf but prioritize the newer one?
Which Sturdy Leaf do you want me to release?
```

A vague statement must not cause a downgrade until the user answers.

### Sturdy Leaf Release

A Sturdy Leaf Release is the user-authorized process for letting a Sturdy Leaf leave high-priority retention.

A confirmed Sturdy Leaf Release changes the state directly:

```text
Sturdy Leaf → Loose Leaf → Leaf Litter
```

The released Leaf does not pass through normal Leaf state unless the user specifically requests normal retention.

The Loose Leaf remains available during its applicable release or grace period.

During that period, the user may restore it to normal or Sturdy state.

When the Loose retention period ends, the Leaf falls into Leaf Litter.

The release record should preserve:

- Original Sturdy Leaf identifier
- User and Tree
- Original scope
- Original protection level
- User phrase or confirmation
- Release time
- Loose retention or grace period
- Fall time
- Resulting Leaf Litter identifier
- Undo or restoration status
- Newer Leaf or instruction that replaced it, when applicable

A Sturdy Leaf Release does not automatically lower the Leaf's sensitivity classification.

A Sturdy Tar Sap Leaf remains Tar Sap protected while Loose and after entering Leaf Litter.

### Promotion Is Easier Than Demotion

A Tree may promote a normal Leaf to Sturdy when the information appears important to future authorized operations and the Tree has permission to retain it.

A Tree may ask before promoting when the material is highly sensitive, broadly scoped, expensive to preserve, or likely to affect many future decisions.

A Tree may not use this discretion in reverse.

A Tree's belief that a Sturdy Leaf is old, stale, contradictory, inconvenient, or less useful is not permission to downgrade it.

The Tree may instead:

- Prioritize newer Leaves
- Mark the Sturdy Leaf as superseded but preserved
- Ask the user whether to release it
- Keep both Leaves with dates and scope
- Explain the conflict through Leaf Foliage

A Tree may automatically demote its own ordinary non-Sturdy Tree-generated Leaves when policy permits.

A Tree must preserve explicit Loose boundaries and user-marked Sturdy Leaves.

Sensitive Leaves use the strongest required local protection in every state.

### Leaf State Commands

```text
Make this a Loose Leaf.
Treat these Leaves as temporary.
Keep these Leaves separate from normal operations.
Return this Loose Leaf to normal.
Make this a normal Leaf.
Make this Leaf sturdy.
Make sure to remember this.
Show why this Leaf changed state.
Show all Sturdy Leaves.
Demote this Sturdy Leaf to a normal Leaf.
You can forget that now.
I think it's time to turn a new Leaf.
Use the newer Leaf and ask me whether to shed the old one.
Shed that Sturdy Leaf.
Keep the old Leaf but prioritize the newer one.
Restore that released Leaf.
```

### Leaves, Solid Material, and Fluids

A Leaf is information held by a Tree.

A Leaf is also a form of Solid Material because it is a bounded information object with identity, state, provenance, and protection.

A Tree may expose an approved whole Leaf or Piece of a Leaf through the Canopy as Solid Material.

The receiving user or Tree may view or access that Leaf without absorbing it.

When approved information must become transferable meaning for absorption, the Tree converts selected information into Water, Sap, Light Syrup, Syrup, Molasses, or another authorized Fluid.

The original Leaf may remain internal while:

- Solid Material access exposes the approved object without absorption
- A protected reference points to the Leaf
- A Fluid carries selected transferable meaning
- An explicit copy creates a destination object or file

The Tree should be able to explain:

- Which Leaf supplied or exposed the information
- Whether the destination viewed, referenced, copied, downloaded, or absorbed it
- Why Solid Material or Fluid form was selected
- What information was included
- What information was withheld
- Which Tree or destination received access
- Whether Moss was generated

### Leaf Commands

```text
Show this Leaf.
Show the Leaves created from my prompt.
Show the Leaves created from your answer.
Show which source generated this Leaf.
Show this Leaf's state.
Show what Fluid this Leaf became.
Grow more from this Leaf.
Shed this Leaf.
Show all Loose Leaves.
Show all Sturdy Leaves.
Make this a Loose Leaf.
Make this a normal Leaf.
Make this Leaf sturdy.
Show User Input Leaves.
Show User Output Leaves.
Show Tree Input Leaves.
Show Tree Output Leaves.
Show what Sunlight or Fertilizer grew this User Output Leaf.
Show which external source created this Tree Input Leaf.
```

### Leaves Created Through Mycelium

Mycelium may access a source without causing the Tree to save the complete source as Leaves.

The Tree should normally create only minimal Mycelial Trace Leaves describing the source, Path, permission, task, result, and method for finding the source again.

The Tree may create fuller Leaves only when the result must be retained, learned from, converted into a Fluid, or used for later growth under the applicable permissions.

### Leaves and Canopy Transit

Ordinary Canopy transit and Solid Material viewing do not create new content Leaves in intermediate or receiving Trees.

A Tree may hold temporary Canopy View Context while connected.

The Canopy may retain Moss to remember a connection, Canopy Section, website, application, protected reference, settings, preferences, last position, or resume checkpoint.

Moss works like a cookie and is Solid Material rather than a Loose Leaf.

The origin and final destination may retain endpoint Leaves or Action Receipts according to their policies.

Intermediate Branch hops are not normally preserved as Leaves.

A receiving Tree may use a Canopy Pass, view Solid Material, or use Moss without adding the source content or Moss to its Leaf Foliage or Grain Pattern.

This is an explicit exception within the small portion of authorized sources that do not need to generate full content Leaves.

### Moss and Leaf Creation
