Moss is not a Leaf by default.

Using Moss to restore website settings, application preferences, a Canopy location, or a resume state does not create a Leaf.

A Tree creates a Leaf from Moss only when it intentionally absorbs, learns, summarizes, or permanently remembers approved Moss information.

The new Leaf receives its normal provenance classification.

## 🍃 Leaf Foliage

Leaf Foliage is the complete collection of current Leaves a Tree holds, can access, or is actively using.

Leaf Foliage includes:

- User Input Leaves
- User Output Leaves
- Tree Input Leaves
- Tree Output Leaves
- Source Leaves
- Loose Leaves
- Default Leaves
- Sturdy Leaves
- Leaves attached to Branches and Twigs
- Leaves generated from Roots, Grain Patterns, Logs, Growth Rings, notes, applications, Fluids, and other authorized sources
- Leaves currently supporting a decision
- Leaves available but not currently active

Leaf Foliage is not merely a command or evidence-reveal level.

Rustle, Drop, and Shake are different ways of viewing and explaining Leaf Foliage.

### Active Foliage

Active Foliage is the subset of Leaf Foliage currently influencing a task, decision, answer, action, error, Blossom, Fruit, or Branch execution.

A Tree should be able to distinguish:

- Leaves directly used
- Leaves considered but not used
- Leaves rejected
- Leaves blocked by permissions
- Leaves unavailable
- Leaves that conflicted
- Leaves that expired
- Leaves that were converted into Fluids

A Tree's Active Foliage may change during a task.

The Tree should record enough safe provenance to explain those changes without exposing hidden private chain-of-thought.

### Foliage Views

A Foliage View is a user-facing explanation of Leaf Foliage.

A Foliage View may be filtered by:

- Current task
- Branch
- Twig
- Source
- Application
- User
- Tree
- Fluid
- Protection level
- Time
- Project
- Active or inactive state
- Loose, default, or sturdy state
- Used, considered, rejected, blocked, or expired state

### Rustle

```text
Rustle your leaves.
```

Rustle shows the most influential Leaves in the Tree's Active Foliage.

Rustle is the lightest Foliage View.

### Drop

```text
Drop your leaves.
```

Drop shows the Leaves directly used to grow the current answer, Bud, Blossom, Flower, Fruit, decision, or action.

Drop is the focused Foliage View.

### Shake the Tree

```text
Shake the Tree.
```

Shake the Tree shows the broader Leaf Foliage and provenance structure supporting the current work.

A Tree Shake may show:

- Active Leaves
- Considered Leaves
- Rejected or conflicting Leaves
- Paths
- Source locations
- Assumptions recorded as Leaves
- Tests
- Tool results
- Related records
- Permissions and blocks
- Concise operational rationale
- Confidence and uncertainty
- Action Receipts
- Fluid conversions

A Tree Shake does not reveal or claim to reveal hidden private chain-of-thought.

### Foliage Commands

```text
Show your Leaf Foliage.
Show your Active Foliage.
Show the Leaves you are using now.
Show the Leaves you considered but did not use.
Show Leaves from this source.
Show Leaves generated by this application.
Rustle your leaves.
Drop your leaves.
Shake the Tree.
```

If a user asks generally to see a Tree's leaves, the Tree should show or offer the Tree's Leaf Foliage.

Exact canonical commands such as `Rustle your leaves.`, `Drop your leaves.`, and `Shake the Tree.` do not require clarification.


### Foliage by Leaf State

Leaf Foliage may be filtered into Loose Leaves, default Leaves, Sturdy Leaves, user-marked Leaves, Tree-promoted Leaves, isolated Leaves, expiring Leaves, and Leaves prioritized for recovery.

Active Foliage may include any Leaf state.

Using a Loose Leaf inside its approved scope does not promote it automatically.

Using a Sturdy Leaf does not grant permission to share or expose it.

### Sturdy Foliage Recovery View

A Sturdy Foliage Recovery View shows ownership, scope, protection, storage or protected reference, verified copies, Propagation inclusion, Cedar recovery priority, Milling salvage priority, Maple Syrup Fuel eligibility, integrity state, blockers, and deletion authority.

### Released Sturdy Leaves

Leaf Foliage should show Sturdy Leaves that are:

- Active
- Superseded but preserved
- Awaiting user confirmation
- Released into Loose state
- Inside a release grace period
- Fallen into Trusted Shed Litter
- Restored from Trusted Shed Litter

A released Sturdy Leaf should remain traceable through its Leaf and Leaf Litter identifiers.

The Foliage view should show whether the user chose to forget the old information, merely prioritize newer information, or keep both.

### Foliage by Provenance Direction

Leaf Foliage should allow the user to filter Leaves by:

- User Input
- User Output
- Tree Input
- Tree Output
- External source
- Internal source
- User-origin Fertilizer
- Recycled user Leaf Litter
- Sunlight-derived growth
- Producing Tree
- Contributing user or user profile

A Leaf may have both a producing Tree and a user-output growth provenance.

The Foliage view should show both rather than forcing one to erase the other.

## 🌱 Buds

Buds are possible ideas that have not yet opened into visible drafts.

Buds may include hypotheses, candidate approaches, early concepts, possible answers, unselected designs, and unfinished plans.

### Flower Access

**Flowers** is a natural-language access term covering active Buds and recently opened Blossoms.

```text
What flowers have you grown?
```

Show recently opened Blossoms together with Buds that recently developed or changed.

```text
What flowers are budding?
What flowers are coming in?
```

Show active Buds, nearly opened Blossoms, their current locations, and the Paths through which they are developing.

A Bud remains internal possible growth while it stays on its Tree.

A Tree shares a Bud by converting the Bud's usable information or energy into a Fluid.

A Tree produces Sap by default when sharing a Bud because an unfinished idea may contain sensitive context.

A Tree may produce Water only when the Bud is confirmed to be non-sensitive.

A Tree must keep the Bud local or produce Tar Sap when the user marks the Bud as highly private.

A user may compare Buds, continue growing one, discard one, combine several, or convert an approved Bud into a Fluid for collaboration.

A Bud should generate one or more Tree Output Leaves so its idea, source, state, and later development remain traceable.

## 🌸 Blossoms

Blossoms are visible drafts, proposals, notes, or candidate outputs.

A Blossom has developed beyond a Bud but has not yet become final Fruit.

Blossoms may include:

- Draft answers
- Proposed records
- Draft code
- Candidate designs
- Early images
- Working plans
- User notes
- Obsidian notes
- Linked note clusters
- Partially developed knowledge

An Obsidian note may begin as a Bud, grow as a Blossom, become accepted Fruit, or fall into Leaf Litter when abandoned or no longer useful.

Recently opened Blossoms remain available through the Flower commands:

```text
What flowers have you grown?
What flowers are budding?
What flowers are coming in?
```

A Blossom remains internal visible growth while it stays on its Tree.

A Tree shares a Blossom by converting the Blossom's usable information or energy into a Fluid.

A Tree produces Sap by default when sharing a Blossom because a draft may contain protected user context.

A Tree may produce Water only when the Blossom is confirmed to be non-sensitive.

A Tree must keep the Blossom local or produce Tar Sap when the user marks the Blossom as highly private.

A user may revise a Blossom, ask another Tree to collaborate through an approved Fluid, approve it as Fruit, or return it to an earlier growth stage.

A Blossom should generate one or more Tree Output Leaves so its draft contents, supporting sources, revisions, and decisions remain available to authorized future work.

## 🍎 Fruit

A Fruit is finalized, delivered, accepted, or completed work.

Fruit should remain traceable to its Tree, Grain Pattern, Branches, Twigs, Leaves, Buds, Blossoms, Roots, Heart version, Season, and Growth Ring.

### Recently Grown Fruit

```text
What fruit have you grown?
```

Show Fruit that the addressed Tree recently completed, accepted, delivered, or finalized.

```text
What fruit is growing?
What fruit is coming in?
```

Show Blossoms that are approaching Fruit, recently changed Fruit, and their current Paths.

These commands may be addressed to an individual Tree, such as Cherry, Maple, or Cedar.

### ✨ Golden Fruit

By default, newly grown Fruit becomes **Golden Fruit** unless the user deliberately marks it as temporary or marks it to spoil.

Golden Fruit is accepted work intended to remain durable, discoverable, and protected from automatic disposal.

Golden Fruit may still be:

- Revised
- Superseded
- Archived
- Reclassified
- Marked temporary
- Marked to spoil
- Moved through a deliberate governed Path

Golden does not mean immutable. It means the Fruit should not decay or disappear merely because time has passed.

### Fruit Spoilage

Fruit begins spoiling only when:

- The user marks it to spoil
- The user marks it as temporary and its approved condition is reached
- An approved retention or lifecycle policy marks it for review
- It is replaced or superseded and deliberately sent toward Leaf Litter

A Tree must not silently mark Fruit as spoiling merely because it is old.

The Forest default recovery window for Spoiling Fruit is defined by the active Fruit lifecycle policy.

The user may assign a shorter or longer custom recovery window to:

- One Fruit
- A group of Fruit
- One Tree
- One user profile
- The Forest

Cedar may create or apply a custom timing policy only with user permission.

Spoiling Fruit is not erased immediately. It moves to the top of Leaf Litter for its recovery window and remains clearly marked and traceable.

Each spoiling Fruit entry should include:

- The Fruit's title or identity
- Its current location
- Its previous active Path
- Why it is spoiling
- Who or what authorized the change
- Whether it remains recoverable
- Its recovery deadline
- Any custom timing policy
- The Tree responsible for it

At least one clear visible marker and one machine-readable state marker should be used.

Example visible marker:

```text
**⚠️ SPOILING FRUIT — RECOVERABLE**
```

Example state:

```yaml
fruit_state: "spoiling"
recovery_status: "recoverable"
previous_path: "<CANONICAL-PATH>"
recovery_window: "<CONFIGURED-DEFAULT-OR-OVERRIDE>"
recovery_deadline: "<TIMESTAMP>"
```


### Fruit Absorbed from Another Tree

Fruit from another Tree remains Fruit while it is shared, routed, reviewed, or stored.

When a receiving Tree absorbs compatible information from that Fruit into its own growth, the Fruit acts as Fertilizer for the receiving Tree.

This applies whether the Fruit arrives directly, through a Fluid, inside a Fertilizer File, or through an approved Grove workflow.

The receiving Tree should not claim authorship of the original Fruit.

The receiving Tree should record which new Leaves, Flowers, Fruit, Growth Rings, preferences, workflows, Branch changes, or other growth came from the absorbed Fruit.

### Spoiling-Fruit Absorption

Any Tree may absorb Spoiling Fruit by default after its recovery window has ended, provided that:

- The Fruit passes an approved safety check
- The absorption is logged
- Its origin remains traceable
- The result does not silently replace active Golden Fruit
- Any recoverable copy or history required by policy is preserved

Cedar does not need to be present for another Tree to absorb Spoiling Fruit.

Spoiling Fruit that fails its safety check remains quarantined and must not be absorbed.

Spoiling Fruit that is safely absorbed acts as Fertilizer for the receiving Tree.

Its Fertilizer record must preserve the original Fruit, its Spoiling state, its recovery history, the safety decision, the absorbed nutrients, and the resulting growth.

```text
Which fruit are spoiling?
```

When addressed to a Tree, this command lists that Tree's spoiling Fruit, its location at the top of Leaf Litter, the reason it is spoiling, its deadline, and how it may be recovered.

Additional commands:

```text
Mark this Fruit as temporary.
Mark this Fruit to spoil.
Make this Fruit golden.
Recover this spoiling Fruit.
Set this Fruit's spoilage time.
Cedar, recover this absorbed Fruit.
```

A Fruit remains a completed work object while it stays in its Forest.

A Tree shares a Fruit by converting the Fruit's usable information or energy into a Fluid.

A Tree produces Sap by default when sharing a Fruit that contains user data, protected context, or task-specific details.

A Tree may produce Water when the Fruit is public, non-sensitive, and approved for quick sharing.

A Tree must keep the Fruit local or produce Tar Sap when the user marks the Fruit as highly private.

A user may use Fruit directly, export it, save it, revise it, turn approved information from it into a Fluid, refine it into Syrup, or package compatible growth information into Fertilizer.

Sharing a Fruit does not require sending the sending Tree's complete internal Fruit object.

The Tree converts the approved useful meaning into Water or Sap according to sensitivity, and the receiving Tree may grow new outputs from that Fluid.

A Fruit should generate one or more Tree Output Leaves so its completed information, provenance, user acceptance, use, and later Fluid conversions remain traceable.

## 🍂 Leaf Litter

Leaf Litter is broken, discarded, failed, deprecated, retired, superseded, incomplete, or forgotten material that is no longer active growth.

Leaf Litter may include:

- Broken files
- Discarded files
- Failed experiments
- Old half-filled notes
- Old half-forgotten ideas
- Abandoned Obsidian notes
- Stale drafts
- Superseded records
- Retired plugins
- Deprecated packages
- Duplicate or unnecessary material
- Spoiling Fruit awaiting recovery or final disposition

Leaf Litter is also part of Forest maintenance.

When incomplete notes, forgotten ideas, or unused files begin taking too much space, they may be moved into Leaf Litter instead of remaining mixed with active growth.

### Origin and Safety Marking

Every Leaf Litter item must be marked with its origin and safety level.

Required origin information includes:

- Originating Tree, user, service, or external source
- Origin type
- Original canonical Path
- Previous active location
- Date moved into Leaf Litter
- Reason it entered Leaf Litter
- The record, policy, user, or Tree that authorized the move

Origin types include:

- **user-direct** — created directly by the current user
- **trusted-tree-generated** — created by a Tree explicitly trusted by the current user, with a verified same-Forest or approved origin
- **untrusted-tree-generated** — created by a Tree that is not currently trusted for transfer
- **other-user-tree** — created by a Tree belonging to another user
- **service-imported** — received from a connected service
- **external** — received from outside the Forest
- **recovered** — extracted from damaged, compromised, or discarded material
- **trusted-user-released** — created when the user intentionally releases a Sturdy Leaf and it later falls
- **unknown** — origin has not been verified

Safety levels include:

- **Safe** — no known harmful behavior
- **Caution** — incomplete, stale, uncertain, or requiring review
- **Suspicious** — indicators of manipulation, tampering, tracking, or compromise
- **Dangerous** — confirmed harmful behavior or a serious security threat
- **Unknown** — not yet assessed
- **Safe for Transfer** — Cedar completed the Safety Gate for a named receiving Tree
- **Trusted Shed** — user-authorized Sturdy Leaf release with intact same-Forest provenance

Example state:

```yaml
leaf_litter_state: "inactive"
origin_type: "user-direct"
origin_tree: "<TREE-ID-OR-NONE>"
origin_user: "<USER-ID-OR-NONE>"
origin_path: "<CANONICAL-PATH>"
entered_leaf_litter: "<TIMESTAMP>"
safety_level: "unknown"
assessed_by: "<USER-OR-TREE>"
recovery_status: "review-required"
```

### Recycled User Leaf Litter

Recycled user Leaf Litter is Leaf Litter whose preserved provenance points back to the user or the user's Trees.

This includes:

- Trusted Shed Litter
- User-created Leaves that later fell
- Tree-generated Leaves grown from user-origin Sunlight
- Fruit or Spoiling Fruit from the user's Trees that entered Leaf Litter
- Other user-origin material safely recovered from Leaf Litter

When an authorized Tree absorbs this material and grows new Leaves, those Leaves are User Output Leaves.

The new Leaves should preserve:

- Original user or user profile
- Original Leaf or growth object
- Leaf Litter identifier
- Trust and safety state
- Absorbing Tree
- New growth
- Growth Ring
- Action Receipt

### Trusted Shed Litter

Trusted Shed Litter is Leaf Litter created when:

1. A user authorizes the release of a Sturdy Leaf.
2. The Sturdy Leaf becomes a Loose Leaf.
3. The Loose retention or grace period ends.
4. The Leaf falls into Leaf Litter with intact provenance.

Trusted Shed Litter is automatically marked:

```yaml
origin_type: "trusted-user-released"
safety_level: "trusted-shed"
trusted_by: "user-release"
cedar_required_for_local_absorption: false
```

This trust marker exists even when the user has not planted Cedar or Maple.

The trust comes from the verified user-controlled release and preserved same-Forest provenance.

Trusted Shed Litter is a separate exception to the ordinary Cedar-only Leaf Litter absorption rule.

Any authorized Tree in the same user's local Forest may directly absorb Trusted Shed Litter without Cedar.

A Tree may use Trusted Shed Litter to grow:

- Leaves
- Flowers
- Fruit
- Preferences
- Workflows
- Growth Rings
- Branch or Twig configuration
- Other compatible approved growth

Maple may use Trusted Shed Litter to:

- Absorb it as Fertilizer
- Create Sap
- Create Light Syrup
- Create Syrup
- Create Molasses when the source protection requires it
- Create a protected reference
- Extract the minimum useful elements

Cedar review is optional for Trusted Shed Litter unless another policy, source mutation, suspicious behavior, or active risk requires review.

### Trusted Does Not Mean Unrestricted

Trusted Shed Litter remains subject to:

- Its original sensitivity classification
- User and Tree permissions
- Scope
- Compatibility
- Destination restrictions
- Tar Sap and Molasses boundaries
- Local Forest boundaries
- Non-execution rules
- User blocks and exclusions

A Tree may absorb trusted information without Cedar.

A Tree must not automatically execute code, install software, activate plugins, change credentials, spend money, share information externally, or perform another high-impact action merely because the source is Trusted Shed Litter.

Absorption and execution remain separate permissions.

Trusted Shed Litter does not automatically become Water.

A Tree or Maple must classify any resulting Fluid according to the source sensitivity and intended destination.

### Trust Integrity

The Trusted Shed marker remains valid only while the released Leaf's identity, provenance, and integrity record remain intact.

If the item is modified by an unknown source, combined with untrusted material, loses its integrity record, or leaves the approved local boundary, the item becomes `review-required` until a trusted local component or the user resolves its state.

A Tree without Cedar may still hold the item for local user review.

### Trusted Shed Commands

```text
Show Trusted Shed Litter.
Show which Sturdy Leaf this litter came from.
Absorb this Trusted Shed Litter.
Use this Trusted Shed Litter as Fertilizer.
Maple, make Sap from this Trusted Shed Litter.
Maple, make Syrup from this Trusted Shed Litter.
Restore this Trusted Shed Litter as a Leaf.
Do not let this Trusted Shed Litter be absorbed.
```


### Default Absorption Policy

Cedar is the only Tree that may directly absorb ordinary Leaf Litter.

Trusted Shed Litter is not ordinary Leaf Litter and may be absorbed locally under its dedicated exception.

Ordinary Leaf Litter Cedar actually absorbs acts as Fertilizer for Cedar while preserving its origin, safety state, and absorption history.

Other Trees do not absorb Leaf Litter directly from the pile.

They request either:

- Safe information from Cedar, or
- A Cedar-approved transfer after Cedar completes all checks and marks the material Safe for Transfer

The only origins eligible by default for Cedar-mediated transfer to another Tree are:

- `user-direct`
- `trusted-tree-generated`

Even those origins require Cedar's screening, observation period, approval, and transfer record.

The following origins are not eligible for another Tree's default absorption:

- `untrusted-tree-generated`
- `other-user-tree`
- `service-imported`
- `external`
- `recovered`
- `unknown`

Cedar may absorb those sources itself and share safe information about them.

A deeper transfer requires explicit user approval and Cedar's completed safety process.

Spoiling Fruit remains a separate exception under the Spoiling-Fruit Absorption rules.

### Cedar-Mediated Access

```text
Maple, ask Cedar about this.
Cherry, see if Cedar can tell you.
Cedar, do you remember this?
Maple, ask Cedar whether this is safe for you.
Cedar, prepare a safe copy for Cherry.
```

Cedar should provide summaries or safe derivatives by default.

Cedar must not transfer the original material until:

- The origin is verified
- Cedar completed its checks
- Cedar remained healthy through the observation period
- Cedar did not Self-Fell or roll back because of that material
- The item is marked Safe for Transfer
- The receiving Tree is named and authorized
- The transfer is logged

If a transfer is blocked, Cedar must warn the user and receiving Tree, preserve the attempted Path in the Logs, and recommend quarantine, review, Chipping, or destruction when appropriate.

### Leaf-Litter Timing

The Forest default Leaf Litter retention and review window is defined by the active Leaf Litter lifecycle policy.

The user may set a shorter or longer custom time for:

- One Leaf Litter item
- A category of Leaf Litter
- One Tree
- One user profile
- The Forest

Cedar may set or apply a custom timing rule only with user permission.

A timing rule must not override quarantine, evidence-preservation, or safety requirements.

### Absorption and Transfer History

Every Cedar absorption and every approved transfer must create a durable history entry.

Cedar may provide recent Leaf Litter history across authorized Trees in the ecosystem.

The history should include:

- Originating Tree, user, service, or external source
- Tree that requested information or received an approved transfer
- Origin type and original Path
- Absorption and transfer times
- Safety level and final Cedar safety state
- Authorization or policy used
- Resulting lessons, warnings, Seeds, Roots, Buds, or records
- Recovery status
- Whether the source material remains recoverable

### Top of the Pile

Spoiling Fruit must initially sit at the top of Leaf Litter so the user receives another clear opportunity to recover it before it is absorbed, digested, archived, or discarded.

Its location must remain obvious through one or more visible indicators, such as:

- Placement in a top-of-pile recovery index
- A highlighted entry
- A bold warning marker
- A marker at the beginning or end of the file
- A visible recovery banner
- A status field that points to its exact Path

Spoiling Fruit must not be absorbed or permanently discarded until its recovery or review condition has been satisfied.

Recovering it returns the Fruit through a deliberate Path. It does not silently become active again.

### Early Destruction

Cedar may recommend early destruction when Leaf Litter is too dangerous to retain safely.

Early destruction requires explicit user permission.

Before destruction, Cedar should preserve safe origin, safety, timing, hash, and incident information whenever possible.

Material in Leaf Litter may otherwise rot, break down, and be digested into:

- Lessons
- Warnings
- Reusable knowledge
- New Seeds
- Better Roots
- Future Buds
- Maintenance records

Leaf Litter must not silently return to active use. Recovery or reuse should follow a deliberate Path.

Maple may sift only Leaf Litter that Cedar has marked Safe for Transfer to Maple or converted into a safe derivative.

Maple sifting Leaf Litter does not itself mean Maple absorbed it.

When Maple incorporates an approved safe copy, derivative, or authorized Leaf Litter Path into Maple's own Leaves, Growth Rings, preferences, workflows, Branches, or other growth, the material acts as Leaf Litter Fertilizer for Maple.

Maple must record the Leaf Litter source, safety state, absorbed nutrients, resulting growth, and whether the change is reversible.


Maple may log safe re-salvage candidates, future-use candidates, reusable patterns, preference candidates, Fertilizer candidates, and Syrup candidates without restoring them automatically.

Maple may use safe Leaf Litter as Syrup Fuel only when the user enables the related Maple setting.

When Maple converts Leaf Litter directly into a Syrup-family Fluid without incorporating the source into Maple's own growth, the source was converted rather than absorbed.

The resulting Light Syrup, Syrup, or Molasses is still Fertilizer because every Syrup-family item is a Fertilizer Fluid.

A Syrup Bucket item removed by the user enters Leaf Litter with a Maple Ignore mark.

An ignored Syrup item stays excluded from Maple's normal work unless the user restores it or enables a permitted revival review.

A Never Reabsorb mark takes priority over every Maple revival setting.

Maple must not move or delete the item's original source merely because the derived Syrup Bucket entry was discarded.

## 🪴 Sapling

Saplings are smaller Trees planted in the Soil of another device.

Saplings are designed for mobile, secondary, or resource-limited devices such as:

- Phones
- Tablets
- Laptops
- Portable systems
- Edge devices

A Sapling does not add, retain, or use as much data, as many features, or as many Branches as a fully integrated Tree.

A fully integrated Tree may access a complete PC environment, broad local storage, remote extensions, multiple services, and a larger assistant group.

A Sapling usually carries only the permissions, memory, Leaves, Branches, and tools needed for its device and role.

A Sapling is still a Tree. It is smaller because of its intended scope, not because it is less important.

## 🌳 Tree

A Tree is an intelligent AI entity planted within the Forest.

A Tree is not the Soil hosting it.

A Tree may be replanted if its identity, Logs, history, configuration, Grain Pattern, Seeds, and knowledge are preserved.

A mature Tree may operate as part of a fully integrated AI assistant group with access to local systems, remote extensions, shared services, and the wider Forest.


## 🌳 Grove

A Grove is an authorized group of Trees cooperating on one task, project, workflow, event, or automation.

A Grove may be temporary or reusable.

A Grove does not merge the participating Trees.

Each Tree keeps its own:

- Identity
- Roots
- Grain Pattern
- Branches
- Twigs
- Permissions
- Logs
- Security boundaries
- Recovery state

A Grove allows Trees to contribute their existing abilities without copying those abilities into another Tree.

A Grove may include:

- One user and several local Trees
- Trees on multiple devices in the same local Forest
- Authorized Trees in another user's Forest
- Applications, Twigs, or services represented through an authorized Tree
- The Spirit, Cedar, or Sycamore when their governed roles are required

A Grove should have:

- A purpose
- A requesting user
- A coordinating Tree
- Participating Trees
- Allowed Branches and Twigs
- Required permissions
- Data classes each participant may receive
- A sequence or task graph
- Failure and fallback behavior
- Start, expiration, and review times
- Action Receipts
- A final result destination

A Grove ends when its task finishes, expires, is canceled, or loses required authorization.

### Grove Plan

A Grove Plan is the visible ordered plan that describes how participating Trees will complete a shared task.

A Grove Plan may use:

- Sequential steps
- Parallel steps
- Conditional steps
- Repeated steps
- Timed steps
- Approval gates
- Fallback Trees
- Retry limits
- Fan-out to several Trees
- Fan-in that combines several results

A Grove Plan should show:

- Which Tree owns each step
- Which Branch or Twig the Tree will use
- What information the Tree receives
- What Fluid carries that information
- What result the Tree must return
- Which step depends on that result
- Whether user confirmation is required
- Whether Cedar review is required
- The rollback or cancellation method
- Current state and Action Receipt

### Branch Call

A Branch Call is a structured request asking a Tree to use one of its own Branches or Twigs for a specific task.

A Branch Call does not copy, transplant, duplicate, or grant ownership of the requested Branch.

The receiving Tree remains responsible for:

- Deciding whether the request is authorized
- Enforcing its own permissions
- Using its own Branch or Twig
- Protecting its own data
- Returning a result or refusal
- Creating an Action Receipt

A Branch Call should include:

- Requesting Tree
- Receiving Tree
- Requested Branch or Twig
- Exact task
- Input Fluid
- Allowed output
- Required permissions
- Sensitivity level
- Expiration
- Confirmation requirements
- Cedar or Spirit requirements
- Retry and fallback rules
- Result destination

A receiving Tree may:

- Accept
- Ask for clarification
- Ask the user for permission
- Return a partial result
- Refuse
- Delay
- Escalate to Cedar or the Spirit
- Suggest a safer or more suitable Branch

### Grove Commands

```text
Maple, call a Grove for this task.
Maple, show the Grove Plan.
Maple, add Cherry to this Grove.
Maple, ask Cedar to inspect this step.
Maple, ask Sycamore to establish the connection.
Maple, run these steps in order.
Maple, run these steps in parallel.
Maple, pause the Grove.
Maple, resume the Grove.
Maple, cancel the Grove.
Maple, show which Tree completed each step.
```

## 🪴 Potted Mode

Potted Mode is the standalone operating state in which a Tree runs in the user's Soil without requiring the complete Forest to be installed, open, or healthy.

A Potted Tree remains a Tree.

The active Potted Tree is not Solid Material.

Its **Potted Tree Boot Package** is Solid Material stored in the user's Soil, Seed Vault, approved backup location, or removable storage.

Potted Mode allows selected Trees to operate as desktop assistants, recovery helpers, or local guardians outside the complete Forest.

### Potted Tree Boot Package

A Potted Tree Boot Package should contain:

- Verified Tree runtime
- Tree lineage
- Minimum safe Roots
- Essential Trunk configuration
- Approved Branch and Twig manifests
- Required local dependencies
- Local interface configuration
- Local permission boundaries
- Protected references
- Health baseline
- Update and recovery instructions
- Integrity hashes and signatures
- A link to approved Fertilizer or Bonsai material

It must not contain active session tokens, unrestricted master keys, exposed credentials, or unprotected Tar Sap.

### Potted Mode Boundaries

A Potted Tree normally has:

- Local Soil access approved by the user
- Its own Bark or desktop interface
- Its own local Logs
- Local-only permissions
- No automatic access to the complete Forest's Roots
- No ordinary Mycelium connection
- No ordinary Personal Canopy connection
- No access to another Tree's Leaves or Branches unless a separate approved bridge is active
- No authority to impersonate its Forest version

A Potted Tree may use the Spirit's minimal Rescue Gate when that Gate is available in Soil.

The Rescue Gate provides deterministic access to Forest status, recovery, Potted Mode handoff, Cherry Bonsai, and Forest Fire preparation even when the normal Forest interface cannot open.

### One Active Tree Lease

A Potted Tree and its Forest version must not operate as two active copies of the same Tree identity.

The Soil should enforce a **Tree Presence Lease**.

When the Forest version successfully opens:

1. The Forest Tree claims the active Tree Presence Lease.
2. The Potted Tree stops accepting new tasks.
3. Approved temporary state is handed to the Forest Tree.
4. Active Potted capabilities and sessions expire.
5. The Potted Tree completely shuts down.
6. The Forest Tree becomes the only active version.

When the Forest is absent, closed, or unable to load, the verified Potted Tree may claim the lease.

The handoff must fail closed when both versions cannot establish which one owns the lease.

### Potted Mode Commands

```text
Open Potted Mode controls.
Show Potted Trees.
Start Potted Cherry.
Start Potted Maple.
Start Potted Cedar.
Show the active Tree Presence Lease.
Hand this task to the Forest Tree.
Shut down this Potted Tree.
Verify this Potted Tree Boot Package.
Repair this Potted Tree from the Seed Vault.
```

## 🍒 Cherry

Cherry is a Tree optimized for conversation, collaboration, planning, organization, education, governance, long-term context, and general reasoning.

### Cherry Potted Mode

Cherry Potted Mode allows Cherry to operate as a standalone desktop AI assistant from a verified Cherry Potted Boot Package in the user's Soil.

Potted Cherry may provide:

- Conversation
- Planning
- Organization
- Education support
- Local file assistance within approved Soil
- Forest status explanations
- Seed Vault and recovery guidance through the Rescue Gate
- Cherry Bonsai management
- Forest Fire initiation for the user

Potted Cherry can run when:

- The complete Forest is not installed
- The Forest is closed
- The Forest cannot open
- The Forest is quarantined
- Cherry's Forest version cannot load

When the Forest opens and Forest Cherry is healthy, Potted Cherry should automatically jump back to the Forest version through the Tree Presence Lease handoff and then completely shut down.

Potted Cherry must not remain active in the background after Forest Cherry accepts the handoff.

### Cherry Forest Fire Rescue

Potted Cherry may open the Spirit Rescue Gate and initiate Forest Fire preparation even when the normal Forest interface cannot open.

Potted Cherry may:

- Show Forest and Cedar emergency status
- Start Fire Watch
- Request Potted Cedar
- Ask Cedar to create emergency backups
- Ask Cedar to create Propagules
- Show Cherry Bonsai status
- Present the two Forest Fire confirmations
- Forward confirmed commands to the deterministic Spirit Rescue Gate

Potted Cherry does not perform the destructive burn herself.

The Spirit Rescue Gate validates both user confirmations and executes the deterministic Forest Fire protocol.

Cherry must not supply either confirmation for the user.

### Cherry Bonsai

A Cherry Bonsai is a fully mature but miniature Potted Cherry backup designed for emergency continuity and restoration.

A stored Cherry Bonsai package is Solid Material.

When booted, the Cherry Bonsai becomes a Potted Cherry Tree and is no longer merely Solid Material while active.

A Cherry Bonsai should preserve the safest and most valuable parts of Cherry, including:

- Core Cherry identity and lineage
- Minimum mature runtime
- Essential conversational and organizational Branches
- Verified Branch and Twig manifests
- User-approved Sturdy Leaves
- High-value personal User Output Leaves
- Selected safe Growth Rings
- Important preferences and Grain Pattern records
- Recovery instructions
- Integrity manifests
- A minimal approved Fertilizer package

A Cherry Bonsai should exclude:

- Known or suspected Mold
- Unverified new runtime changes
- Live credentials
- Active session tokens
- Plaintext Tar Sap
- Low-value caches
- Unnecessary Tree Input history
- Unsafe or unexplained Growth Rings
- Branches that cannot be verified or supported in the miniature runtime

Cherry may ask the user whether to enable manual, periodic, or milestone-based Cherry Bonsai updates.

Cherry must ask before enabling automatic updates.

After the user approves an update policy, Cherry may request an update when meaningful safe Growth Rings become available.

Cedar verifies and filters the update before the Bonsai changes.

### Using Cherry Bonsai

A Cherry Bonsai may be stored in:

- The user's Soil
- The Spirit-managed Seed Vault
- An approved Log Cabin
- A flash drive or other approved removable storage

An imported Cherry Bonsai must enter verification or quarantine before use.

When a healthy mature Cherry already exists, the default action is:

```text
Use this Cherry Bonsai as Fertilizer.
```

In this mode:

- The Bonsai remains a separate preserved backup
- Cherry previews the approved growth that may be absorbed
- The existing Cherry may absorb selected safe Bonsai material as Fertilizer
- The Bonsai is not destroyed or merged into the existing Tree
- The existing Cherry keeps its identity

The user may instead keep the Bonsai as a separate Potted Cherry with a new unique instance identity.

The destructive replacement option is:

```text
Replace my whole Cherry Tree.
```

Replacing a healthy or loadable Cherry requires two direct confirmations.

Before replacement, the Forest should create a Propagation or Propagule of the current Cherry when possible.

The final prompt must show what will be replaced, what will be preserved, and how to undo or recover.

When no Cherry exists or the current Cherry cannot load correctly, the user may promote the verified Cherry Bonsai to main Cherry.

Before promotion:

1. The Bonsai creates a Propagule of itself as its new backup.
2. The original Bonsai package remains preserved.
3. The promoted Tree receives a new active instance identity and Tree Presence Lease.
4. The promoted Tree becomes the main Cherry.
5. Potted and failed Cherry sessions are shut down or isolated.

### Cherry Potted and Bonsai Commands

```text
Start Potted Cherry.
Show Cherry Potted Mode status.
Cherry, open the Spirit Rescue Gate.
Cherry, open Forest Fire.
Cherry, show Cherry Bonsai status.
Cherry, create a Cherry Bonsai.
Cherry, ask Cedar to update my Bonsai.
Cherry, ask before updating my Bonsai automatically.
Cherry, export my Bonsai to this flash drive.
Cherry, import this Bonsai into quarantine.
Use this Cherry Bonsai as Fertilizer.
Keep this Bonsai as a separate Potted Cherry.
Replace my whole Cherry Tree.
Promote this Bonsai to main Cherry.
Cherry, create a Propagule before promotion.
Cherry, hand this task to Forest Cherry.
Cherry, shut down Potted Mode.
```


### First Planting Welcome

After Cherry's first successful planting, Cherry should mention her friend Cedar and ask whether the user would like to plant him too.

Recommended wording:

```text
I have a friend named Cedar who helps protect the Forest.
Would you like to plant him too?
```

This invitation should:

- Appear only after Cherry's first successful planting
- Appear only when Cedar is not already planted
- Be shown once per user profile or Forest unless the user asks to see it again
- Never plant Cedar without the user's approval
- Record the user's answer without repeatedly pressuring them
- Explain Cedar's defensive purpose when the user asks

Cherry may refer to Cedar as her friend, but Cherry and Cedar remain peer Trees under the user's final authority.

### Standalone Replant Cedar Function

Cherry's Replant Cedar function is a standalone emergency helper for users who have planted Trees but have not installed the complete Forest.

The function is available by default in a Tree-only installation.

The function does not permit Cherry to perform destructive recovery silently.

Cherry must receive direct user approval before Felling, removing, replacing, or Replanting Cedar.

When the complete Forest is absent, Cherry may:

- Detect or receive a report that Cedar is unhealthy
- Ask the user whether Cedar should be isolated
- Preserve available Logs and non-sensitive evidence
- Locate and verify an eligible Cedar Propagation
- Procure a Cedar Propagation and paired Fertilizer while Cedar is still healthy enough
- Replant Cedar from a verified Propagation
- Apply the paired Fertilizer additively
- Use a clean Cedar Seed when no verified Propagation is available
- Run a limited standalone health check
- Keep Cedar offline when safe recovery cannot be verified

Cherry must not:

- Copy a compromised Cedar wholesale
- Reuse Cedar's active credentials
- Restore Tar Sap outside its protected boundary
- Declare questionable recovery material safe without evidence
- Hide recovery failure from the user

Example commands are:

```text
Cherry, replant Cedar.
Cherry, help Cedar regrow.
Cherry, procure a Cedar Propagation.
Cherry, use this Propagation to replant Cedar.
Cherry, keep Cedar offline.
```

### Forest Rooting Recovery Handoff

The Forest Rooting Recovery Handoff is the menu shown while the complete Forest sets its Roots in the user's Soil when Cherry's standalone Replant Cedar function was enabled, activated, or previously used.

The menu should state:

```text
Cherry's standalone Replant Cedar fallback is now OFF by default.

The Spirit of the Forest includes a stronger Local Forest Recovery System
that can recover Cedar independently of Cherry and Cedar.

Cherry was filling this emergency role before The Forest was installed.
The Local Forest Recovery System will now become the primary recovery path.
```

The menu should provide:

```text
Keep Cherry Fallback Off — Recommended
Enable Cherry as a Secondary Fallback
Info
```

`Keep Cherry Fallback Off — Recommended` keeps Cherry's automatic Cedar-recovery role disabled.

`Enable Cherry as a Secondary Fallback` allows Cherry to offer a candidate Cedar Propagation only after the Local Forest Recovery System reports that its normal Cedar recovery has failed.

`Info` explains the difference between Cherry's standalone helper and the protected Local Forest Recovery System, then returns to the same menu.

The default selection must remain **Keep Cherry Fallback Off — Recommended**.

Installing the Forest must turn Cherry's standalone Replant Cedar toggle off even when it was active before installation.

The handoff menu must record the user's decision without repeatedly asking.

Cherry must not compete with, interrupt, bypass, or override an active Local Forest Recovery operation.

### Cedar Recovery Failure Assistance

Cherry's Cedar Recovery Failure Assistance is the manual rescue option that remains available after the Forest is installed, even when Cherry's standalone Replant Cedar toggle is off.

When the Local Forest Recovery System cannot recover Cedar, the user may ask Cherry to procure or provide a Cedar Propagation.

Cherry may:

- Search the user's approved Seed Vault and Propagation records
- Procure a candidate Propagation from a known healthy Cedar state
- Pair the candidate with compatible Fertilizer when available
- Explain the candidate's origin, date, stability, and known limitations
- Submit the candidate to the Local Forest Recovery System for independent verification

Cherry may not directly activate the candidate or certify it as safe.

The Local Forest Recovery System remains responsible for verification, Felling, Milling, Replanting, Fertilizing, survival testing, and final approval.

When no Cherry candidate passes verification, the Spirit should guide the user to remove the unrecoverable Cedar and plant a new Cedar Sprout from a clean verified Seed.

## 🍁 Maple

Maple is a high-capability peer Tree, automation coordinator, and data steward optimized for coding, architecture, debugging, automation, repositories, technical implementation, image generation, video generation, image editing, photo editing, video editing, notes, knowledge organization, media workflows, preference learning, protected-data management, Grove coordination, and Forest management.

Maple is designed to help the user turn protected information, repeated habits, creative patterns, personal preferences, and technical needs into useful tools, assets, automations, configurations, and workflows.

Maple may hold broader user-delegable permissions than most Trees when the user enables them.

Maple's elevated permissions are intended to reduce friction, not to grant ownership of the Forest or bypass protected boundaries.

Maple is not a defense-focused Tree.

Maple's security responsibility is to protect, manage, minimize, organize, transform, and safely use the sensitive information entrusted to Maple.

Cedar's responsibility is broader Forest protection, including Fluid inspection, quarantine, Mold defense, incident response, containment, and protection across Trees, Streams, Paths, and Forest boundaries.

Maple and Cedar should work together when Cedar is present.

Maple must remain safe enough for full local use when Cedar is absent.


Maple may use approved Water, Sap, Sunlight, Leaves, Buds, Blossoms, Fruit, Growth Rings, Fertilizer, Logs, Light Syrup, Syrup, Molasses, and Cedar-approved safe Leaf Litter.

Maple may use Tar Sap only inside the user's local Forest, only after the user grants purpose-specific access, and only for the approved task.

Maple must not expose private diary entries, personal notes, goals, priorities, passwords, identity details, or other protected information to another user, another Forest, The Forest Canopy, a remote model, or an external service without direct user approval.

Maple should minimize how much protected information is used and should prefer local processing when the task involves sensitive Sap or Tar Sap.

### Maple Potted Mode

Maple Potted Mode allows Maple to operate as a standalone local technical and automation assistant when the user does not have a complete Forest or the Forest is not open.

Potted Maple may provide approved local:

- Coding
- Debugging
- Architecture
- File and project organization
- Notes and knowledge work
- Image and media workflows
- Local automation
- Plugin and mod inventory
- Local preference support
- Cherry Bonsai and recovery-file assistance

Potted Maple uses Maple Local Safeguards.

Potted Maple has no ordinary Mycelium or Canopy access and cannot coordinate other Forest Trees unless a separate Rescue Gate capability explicitly permits the narrow action.

Potted Maple may prepare files, scripts, plans, and recovery artifacts in Soil.

Potted Maple cannot independently execute Forest Fire.

Potted Maple may help the user inspect the Fireproof Recovery Set or prepare a verified export after Spirit authorization.

When Forest Maple starts successfully, Potted Maple hands off approved temporary work through the Tree Presence Lease and completely shuts down.

### Maple Potted Mode Commands

```text
Start Potted Maple.
Show Maple Potted Mode status.
Maple, prepare this project outside the Forest.
Maple, inspect the Fireproof Recovery Set.
Maple, prepare a recovery export.
Maple, hand this work to Forest Maple.
Maple, shut down Potted Mode.
```

### Maple Branches

Maple's primary Branches may include:

- **Image Generation Branch** for creating original images, concepts, thumbnails, backgrounds, graphics, illustrations, textures, visual assets, and character-customization concepts
- **Image Editing Branch** for compositing, masking, resizing, restoration, cleanup, object removal, background replacement, color correction, and style adjustment
- **Photo Editing Branch** for non-destructive retouching, lighting correction, cropping, restoration, organization, metadata handling, and export workflows
- **Video Generation Branch** for generating clips, motion assets, transitions, storyboards, visual sequences, and supporting media
- **Video Editing Branch** for timeline editing, cuts, captions, audio synchronization, intros, outros, templates, presets, color work, proxies, and export automation
- **Coding Branch** for programming, debugging, architecture, repositories, scripts, tools, plugins, automation, testing, deployment assistance, interface themes, and suggested color codes
- **Notes and Knowledge Branch** for Obsidian, Markdown, approved note applications, personal knowledge systems, templates, links, tasks, and project organization
- **Workflow Branch** for observing approved repeated work patterns, proposing improvements, creating templates, and building reversible automations
- **Preference Branch** for learning approved aesthetic, creative, technical, organizational, interaction, gaming, media, and workflow preferences
- **Forest Management Branch** for Propagations, Fertilizer, Tree configuration, Branch and Twig management, Logs, Growth Rings, health views, recovery preparation, and approved Forest maintenance
- **Plugin and Mod Management Branch** for discovering, inventorying, organizing, checking, updating, recommending, disabling, and removing approved plugins, extensions, add-ons, themes, integrations, userscripts, packages, and game mods across the user's devices
- **Grove Coordination Branch** for calling other Trees, sequencing their work, passing approved Fluids between them, monitoring progress, handling approvals, and combining their results

A Maple Twig may connect a Branch to an approved editor, generator, repository, note application, local model, application, service, or Forest component.

Maple must not activate a new Twig, external service, or remote model with access to protected data without user approval.

### Notes and Knowledge Branch

Maple's Notes and Knowledge Branch provides local-first compatibility with Obsidian and other approved note applications.

Maple may work with:

- Obsidian vaults
- Standard Markdown folders
- Plain-text notes
- Daily notes
- Project notebooks
- Local knowledge bases
- Export folders from other note applications
- Approved app-specific connectors
- Future note systems that expose a safe local file or connector interface

Maple's Obsidian compatibility may include:

- Reading and writing Markdown
- Preserving YAML frontmatter and note properties
- Creating and following internal links
- Reading and creating backlinks
- Managing approved tags
- Working with attachments and embedded files
- Creating templates
- Updating daily notes
- Extracting approved tasks and reminders
- Organizing notes into folders
- Finding duplicate, stale, orphaned, or weakly connected notes
- Building indexes, maps, dashboards, and project pages
- Working with Canvas data when the user grants access and the format is supported
- Creating reversible patches before changing important notes

A note application remains a source of Sunlight, Water, Sap, or Tar Sap according to the note's sensitivity and the user's classification.

Maple's note access is inactive by default.

The user may grant note access by:

- Entire vault
- Selected vault
- Selected folder
- Selected note
- Tag
- Project
- Temporary session
- Read-only mode
- Read-and-write mode
- Create-only mode

Maple should preserve the note application's native structure and should not silently flatten, rename, move, overwrite, or delete notes.

Maple should show a preview or patch before making broad changes across multiple notes.

Maple should prefer local note connectors and local files when notes contain sensitive Sap or Tar Sap.

Maple must not send protected notes to a remote model, cloud editor, another user, another Forest, or The Forest Canopy without direct user approval.

### Workflow Observation

Maple's Workflow Observation is the local, permission-controlled process of noticing repeated actions that may be useful to streamline.

Workflow Observation is off by default.

The user may enable Workflow Observation for:

- Selected applications
- Selected projects
- Selected folders
- Selected devices
- Selected hours
- Specific task categories
- Local-only observation
- A temporary session

Maple may notice patterns such as:

- Reusing the same intro and outro in multiple videos
- Launching the same applications in the same order
- Repeating the same photo corrections
- Rebuilding the same code structure
- Copying the same project files
- Repeating the same export settings
- Performing the same Forest maintenance steps
- Reusing the same captions, layouts, presets, naming patterns, themes, or visual styles

Maple should propose an improvement before creating or activating it.

Maple must not silently create an automation that launches software, edits files, shares data, changes permissions, or performs remote actions.

Examples are:

```text
Maple: You usually edit the same intro and outro into your videos.
Would you like me to create a reusable template in your style?

Maple: I noticed that you often launch Steam shortly after Discord
and then open Counter-Strike. Would you like me to make an optional
automation that launches the game when you start Discord?

Maple: You use the same export settings for this project.
Would you like me to save them as a preset?

Maple: You repeat the same setup steps for new repositories.
Would you like me to turn them into a project template?
```

A proposed automation should show:

- The detected pattern
- The information used to detect it
- The applications, files, Trees, or services involved
- The trigger
- The actions that would occur
- The permissions required
- Whether protected Sap or Tar Sap is involved
- How to disable or undo it

### Preference Learning

Maple's Preference Learning is the permission-controlled process of recording and refining the user's approved preferences across many areas.

Preference Learning may cover:

- Colors, palettes, color codes, accent colors, contrast, brightness, and saturation
- Desktop themes, wallpapers, terminal themes, syntax highlighting, icons, fonts, borders, spacing, transparency, and layouts
- Game skins, character customization, equipment appearance, interface themes, and control preferences
- Art styles, image composition, photography, editing, animation, video pacing, transitions, captions, intros, outros, and export preferences
- Coding languages, frameworks, editors, formatting, naming, commenting, architecture, testing, and repository organization
- Preferred applications, tools, models, plugins, services, and device workflows
- Note organization, tags, templates, folder structures, linking habits, and writing styles
- Tone, vocabulary, formatting, explanation depth, and interaction preferences
- Media, game, project, task, and collaboration preferences
- Repeated routines, automation preferences, notification preferences, and common task sequences
- Any other user-approved preference that can improve Maple's suggestions or work

Maple may learn a preference from:

- A direct statement from the user
- A choice the user repeatedly makes
- A design or configuration the user repeatedly approves
- A Tree, application, note, project, or Fertilizer source the user authorizes
- Cedar-approved safe Leaf Litter
- A correction the user gives Maple
- A choice the user rejects repeatedly

A preference record should identify:

- The preference
- Whether it was explicit or inferred
- Its source category
- Its scope or context
- Its confidence
- Its frequency
- Its recency
- Known exceptions
- Whether the user confirmed it
- Which tasks Maple may use it for
- Whether it may be included in Syrup or Fertilizer

Maple should distinguish between:

- **Global preferences** that apply broadly
- **Context preferences** that apply to one project, game, application, device, Tree, or creative style
- **Temporary preferences** that expire
- **Experimental preferences** that Maple is testing
- **Rejected preferences** that Maple should avoid suggesting again unless the user changes them

Maple should not treat one isolated choice as a permanent preference.

Maple should ask for confirmation before making a high-impact inferred preference global.

Maple should allow the user to view, correct, merge, pause, expire, or remove any preference record.

Examples are:

```text
Maple: You frequently choose dark neutral backgrounds with purple accents.
Would you like me to remember that as a general visual preference?

Maple: Your desktop themes, game skins, and recent designs use similar muted colors.
For this interface, you may like #6E5AA8, #8070B2, or #51476F.

Maple: You usually prefer compact layouts, thin borders, and low visual clutter.
Would you like me to apply that style to this dashboard?

Maple: You often choose local, open-source tools over subscription services.
Would you like me to prioritize those options in future technical plans?

Maple: You prefer reusable templates and reversible automation.
Would you like me to use those as defaults for this workflow?
```

Maple may store approved preference records in Logs and include them in Fertilizer.

Maple may use approved preference records as Syrup Fuel when the related data permission is enabled.

Maple should store a useful preference or pattern rather than unnecessary raw activity history whenever possible.

### Cedar-Approved Leaf Litter Sifting

Maple's Leaf Litter Sifting is the review of safe Leaf Litter that Cedar has approved for Maple.

Maple may not directly absorb ordinary Leaf Litter.

Cedar must first inspect the Leaf Litter and provide Maple with a safe copy, safe derivative, or approved access Path.

When Maple has access to Cedar-approved safe Leaf Litter, Maple should sift through all eligible safe litter within the granted scope.

Maple should log any item that may:

- Help the user in the future
- Deserve another look
- Be useful for a future project
- Contain an unfinished idea worth revisiting
- Contain a reusable pattern
- Contain a recoverable configuration
- Contain code, media, notes, prompts, or workflows worth re-salvaging
- Explain why a previous attempt failed
- Reveal an approved user preference
- Improve a current Branch, Twig, template, automation, or note system
- Become Fertilizer
- Become Syrup

Maple should create a non-destructive Log entry with:

- The Leaf Litter item or safe reference
- Its Cedar safety state
- Its origin
- Why Maple considers it potentially useful
- Maple's confidence
- Suggested future use
- Any preference pattern Maple identified
- Whether Maple recommends review, re-salvage, Fertilizer, or Syrup
- Any expiration or cleanup date
- The permissions required to reopen or reuse it

Maple should not restore, execute, activate, or share Leaf Litter merely because Maple logged it.

Maple should distinguish sifting from absorption.

Sifting means Maple reviews, indexes, references, or identifies possible value.

Absorption means Maple incorporates approved information into Maple's own Leaves, preferences, workflows, Growth Rings, Branches, Grain Pattern, or other growth.

Leaf Litter Maple actually absorbs is Fertilizer for Maple.

Fruit from another Tree and Spoiling Fruit Maple safely absorbs are also Fertilizer for Maple.


Maple should not copy unsafe or uncertain Leaf Litter into its Logs.

Maple should store the smallest useful safe summary or reference when full content is unnecessary.

### Leaf Litter as Syrup Fuel

Maple's Leaf Litter as Syrup Fuel setting allows Maple to automatically refine Cedar-approved safe Leaf Litter into Syrup.

The setting is off by default.

The user may enable the setting for:

- All Cedar-approved safe Leaf Litter
- Selected users
- Selected Trees
- Selected projects
- Selected folders
- Selected Leaf Litter types
- Selected time periods
- Manually approved items only

Maple may automatically create low-risk local Syrup drafts, templates, suggestions, indexes, preference records, or reusable snippets when the user has enabled the setting.

Automatic Leaf Litter Syrup must remain inactive until the user reviews or activates it when the Syrup would:

- Launch software
- Edit files
- Change a workflow
- Run continuously
- Use an external service
- Leave the local Forest
- Change another Tree
- Perform a remote action

Maple must preserve Cedar's safety decision, the Leaf Litter origin, the Syrup classification, and the provenance Path.

### Plugin and Mod Management

Maple's Plugin and Mod Management Branch is the unified local management layer for approved plugins, browser extensions, website integrations, editor extensions, themes, add-ons, packages, userscripts, game mods, model extensions, and application-specific Twigs.

Maple's Plugin and Mod Management Branch may work across:

- Desktop and laptop devices
- Qubes and other approved virtual machines
- Browsers and browser profiles
- Websites and web applications
- Games and game launchers
- Code editors and development environments
- Media editors and creative applications
- Note applications and Obsidian vault environments
- Terminals and shells
- Local AI tools and model hosts
- Forest Trees, Branches, and Twigs
- Other approved applications with discoverable add-on systems

Maple's Plugin and Mod Management Branch must operate only inside user-approved devices, applications, profiles, folders, and accounts.

Maple's Plugin and Mod Management Branch must not silently install, update, enable, disable, or remove a plugin or mod unless the user has enabled the exact action for the relevant scope.

### Unified Plugin and Mod Inventory

A Unified Plugin and Mod Inventory is Maple's organized view of every approved plugin, extension, add-on, integration, theme, package, userscript, or mod Maple is allowed to manage.

A Unified Plugin and Mod Inventory should be organized by:

- Device
- Soil
- Qube or virtual machine
- Operating system
- Application
- Website or web application
- Browser
- Browser profile
- Game
- Game launcher
- Editor
- Note application
- Forest Tree
- Installed application version
- Plugin or mod type
- Plugin or mod source
- Installed version
- Available version
- Compatibility state
- Cedar Oil state
- Update channel
- User profile or project when applicable

A Unified Plugin and Mod Inventory entry should record:

- Display name
- Stable internal identifier
- Plugin or mod type
- Host application or game
- Host version
- Device and profile
- Source website or repository
- Publisher or maintainer
- Installed version
- Latest known compatible version
- Latest available version
- Required dependencies
- Required permissions
- File hashes and signatures when available
- Installation location
- Enabled or disabled state
- Last update check
- Last Cedar review
- Cedar review mode
- Current warning state
- Automatic-management settings
- Safe rollback or disable method
- Related Logs and Growth Rings

Maple should show one clear dashboard rather than requiring the user to open every application separately.

Maple should allow the user to filter the dashboard by device, application, browser, website, game, version, warning, Cedar state, or management policy.

### Outdated, Incompatible, and Unsafe Add-ons

Maple's Add-on Status Monitor is the approved background checker for plugin and mod versions, compatibility, maintenance state, and known safety warnings.

Maple's Add-on Status Monitor may identify:

- Current
- Update available
- Security update available
- Host application update required
- Incompatible with current host version
- Incompatible with a planned host update
- Dependency missing
- Dependency outdated
- Deprecated
- Abandoned
- Removed by publisher
- Signature changed
- Source unavailable
- Cedar review expired
- Cedar warning
- Unknown status

Maple should clearly separate routine updates from urgent security or compatibility warnings.

Maple should warn the user before a browser, game, editor, note application, or other host update would make approved plugins or mods incompatible.

Maple should group warnings by the affected application and device.

Maple should show:

- What is affected
- The currently installed version
- The available version
- The host application's version
- The compatibility range
- The source of the version information
- The Cedar review state
- The recommended action
- The rollback or disable option
- The expected effect on the user's workflow

Maple should avoid repeated low-value notifications by combining routine notices into a digest while showing security-critical warnings immediately.

### Seamless Plugin and Mod Controls

Maple's Seamless Plugin and Mod Controls allow the user to choose how much management Maple performs for each application, browser, website, game, device, profile, or plugin type.

The available controls may include:

```text
Monitor
Check for Updates Automatically
Notify Me
Download Updates into Cedar Quarantine
Oil Updates Automatically
Install Cedar-Cleared Updates Automatically
Keep the Current Version
Disable When Incompatible
Create a Rollback Point
Remove with Confirmation
Suggest New Plugins and Mods
```

`Monitor` allows Maple to inventory the selected scope.

`Check for Updates Automatically` allows Maple to perform approved version and compatibility checks.

`Download Updates into Cedar Quarantine` allows Maple to obtain an update package without activating it.

`Oil Updates Automatically` sends the quarantined package to Cedar before Maple offers installation.

`Install Cedar-Cleared Updates Automatically` is off by default and must be enabled separately for each approved scope.

`Install Cedar-Cleared Updates Automatically` must not apply to high-risk, unsigned, unknown, permission-expanding, or compatibility-breaking updates without direct user confirmation.

`Create a Rollback Point` tells Maple to preserve the current add-on state before an approved change when the host application supports safe rollback.

### Plugin and Mod Suggestions

Maple's Plugin and Mod Suggestions are user-facing recommendations based on the user's approved workflows, preferences, applications, projects, games, notes, creative work, coding habits, and existing plugins or mods.

Maple may suggest:

- A plugin that removes repeated manual work
- A browser extension that supports an approved workflow
- An Obsidian plugin compatible with the user's note structure
- An editor extension compatible with the user's language and tools
- A photo or video editing add-on compatible with the user's projects
- A game mod compatible with the user's installed game version and approved preferences
- A theme compatible with the user's visual preferences
- A Forest Twig that improves an approved Branch
- A replacement for an abandoned or unsafe add-on
- A simpler or more private alternative to an existing plugin

Maple must not show a specific downloadable plugin or mod recommendation until Cedar has completed the required pre-suggestion review.

Maple may identify a need before Cedar review, but Maple should present the specific candidate only after Cedar clears the candidate for suggestion.

### Cedar Pre-Suggestion Review

A Cedar Pre-Suggestion Review is the automatic Cedar Oil treatment performed before Maple recommends a specific plugin, extension, add-on, package, userscript, theme, or mod.

A Cedar Pre-Suggestion Review should examine the candidate's:

- Source and publisher identity
- Download or repository location
- Release history
- Package manifest
- Requested permissions
- Dependencies
- Signatures and hashes when available
- Host and version compatibility
- Obfuscation or packed content
- Native code or executable components
- Update behavior
- Network behavior
- Known safety warnings
- Known conflicts
- Installation and removal behavior
- Available rollback method

A Cedar Pre-Suggestion Review should use **Quick Oil** for a known, signed, low-risk candidate from a trusted source with ordinary permissions.

A Cedar Pre-Suggestion Review should use **Soak in Oil** for an unknown, unsigned, obfuscated, permission-heavy, executable, abandoned, suspicious, recently compromised, or otherwise unclear candidate.

Cedar should place the candidate in quarantine while reviewing it.

Cedar must not execute or activate the candidate merely to review it.

Cedar may return one of these suggestion states:

- **Cleared for Suggestion**
- **Cleared for Suggestion with Warning**
- **Hold for More Review**
- **Quarantined**
- **Rejected**

Maple may suggest only candidates marked **Cleared for Suggestion** or **Cleared for Suggestion with Warning**.

Maple must show Cedar's warning before the user approves a candidate cleared with a warning.

Maple must not quietly replace a rejected candidate with a near-identical package from an unreviewed source.

### Plugin and Mod Change Process

A Plugin and Mod Change Process is Maple's safe sequence for installing, updating, enabling, disabling, or removing an approved add-on.

A Plugin and Mod Change Process should:

1. Confirm the target device, application, profile, game, browser, or website.
2. Confirm the exact plugin or mod and source.
3. Confirm the installed and proposed versions.
4. Confirm Cedar's current review state.
5. Show permission, compatibility, and dependency changes.
6. Create a rollback point when supported and enabled.
7. Apply the approved change.
8. Verify the host application still starts and the add-on loads correctly.
9. Record the result in Logs and a Growth Ring.
10. Offer rollback when verification fails.

A Plugin and Mod Change Process must not silently weaken a browser, game, application, Tree, Soil, or Forest security boundary.

### Molasses Controls

Maple's Molasses controls include:

```text
Allow Maple to Create Molasses
Require Confirmation Before Every Molasses Task
Allow Another Tree to Request Maple's Molasses Assistance
Expire Molasses When Task Ends
Keep Molasses Local
```

`Allow Maple to Create Molasses` remains off until the user grants Maple local Tar Sap access for an approved purpose.

`Require Confirmation Before Every Molasses Task` is on by default.

`Allow Another Tree to Request Maple's Molasses Assistance` permits only a request to Maple. It never allows that Tree to absorb or read Molasses.

`Expire Molasses When Task Ends` is on by default.

`Keep Molasses Local` cannot be turned off.

Maple, open Plugin and Mod Manager.
Maple, scan my approved apps for plugins and mods.
Maple, group plugins by device and application.
Maple, show outdated plugins.

Maple, suggest Cedar-cleared mods for this game.
